\ket{\varphi}\approx_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\theta}\otimes\ket{\psi}

(151)

Then

\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\sum_{\Delta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\Delta}\otimes\ket{\varphi}

is
2\epsilon
-invariant under the following operator:

\ket{\Delta}\ket{\theta}\rightarrow\ket{\theta}\ket{\Delta}

(152)

9.3.2
Proof of Theorem
9.1

Proof.

Unrolling the definition of
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
, we only need to prove, for all
i\in[0,L]
,

b\in\{0,1\}
:

{\sf SWAP}^{(i)}_{1-b,b}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\sum_{\Delta_{b}^{(i)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\underbrace{\ket{\Delta_{b}^{(i)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(i)}_{b}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}})\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}

(153)

Denote the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{1-b}
branch of
\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}
as
\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{2.a}}
. Since
{\sf SWAP}^{(i)}_{1-b,b}
only operates nontrivially on
\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{2.a}}
, (
153
) is further reduced to proving

{\sf SWAP}^{(i)}_{1-b,b}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\sum_{\Delta_{b}^{(i)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\Delta_{0}^{(i)}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{2.a}})\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{2.a}}

(154)

Recall the definition of
\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}
in Setup
3
:

\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}={\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{1}}

where
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
is in Setup
1
. Applying Lemma
6.6
we get

\ket{\varphi^{2.a}_{(i),1-b}}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{1}_{(i),1-b}}

(155)

where we use
\ket{\varphi^{1}_{(i),1-b}}
to denote the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1-b}^{(i)}
-branch of
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
.

To prove it, we are going to use Fact
10
, a linear algebra fact that is proved previously for the preparation of this proof. We need to show
\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{2.a}}
is close to a state that satisfies the condition of Fact
10
(that is, does not depend on
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b}
). This is by replacing state and operations in (
155
) by states and operators that does not depend on the values of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b}
step by step:

1.

Suppose the random paddings used within the
{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}
are sampled and stored in client-side register
{\boldsymbol{R}}
. Since
\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{1}}
is efficiently-preparable, by Lemma
3.4
:

\exists\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}_{(i),1-b}}\text{ independent of ${\boldsymbol{H}}(R||\cdots)$ }:\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}_{(i),1-b}}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\varphi^{1}_{(i),1-b}}

(156)

2.

Define
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}
as the adversary that compared to
{\mathsf{Adv}}
, all the queries to
{\boldsymbol{H}}
are replaced by
{\boldsymbol{H}}^{mid}
where
{\boldsymbol{H}}^{mid}
is a blinded oracle where entries in the form of
\{0,1\}^{2\kappa}||x^{(i)}_{b}
are blinded. (Recall the key length of the switch gadget is also
\kappa
.) Intuitively, recall we are studying the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{1-b}
-branch thus
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{b}
is not predictable by the server. Formally, by Lemma
6.5
we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{1}}

(157)

\displaystyle\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi_{(i),1-b}^{1}}

(158)

3.

Combining (
156
)(
158
) above we have

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}_{(i),1-b}}:={\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}_{(i),1-b}}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}_{(i),1-b}}

and by its definition it is independent to random oracle output register
{\boldsymbol{H}}(R||\{0,1\}^{\kappa}||x_{b}^{(i)})
. Recall the encryptions of
\theta_{b}^{(i)}
in the client-side messages of
{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}
have the form

ct^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}=h+\theta_{b}^{(i)},h=H(R^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}||x^{({\text{switch}})}_{b^{({\text{switch}})}}||x_{b}^{(i)})

Thus after the application of
{\sf ReviseRO}^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}
on
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}_{(i),1-b}}
the overall state could be written as

\sum_{\theta^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{h\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}}\underbrace{\ket{ct^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}}}_{{\boldsymbol{ct}}^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}}\underbrace{\ket{\psi_{ct^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}}}}_{\text{other parts}}

That is, the rightmost term does not depend on the value of register
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}
(but could depend on the transcript register
{\boldsymbol{ct}}^{(i)}_{b^{({\text{switch}})},b}
). Now the condition for applying Fact
10
is satisfied. This completes the proof of (
154
).

This completes the proof of (
153
) and completes the whole proof.
∎

9.4
New Set-up

Now we are going to formalize a new setup that captures the properties of states after the randomization of
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
.

Set-up 4
.

Define Setup
4
as the set of states that could be written as

{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\varphi}),\ket{\varphi}\text{ is in Setup \ref{setup:3}}.

Since there have been lots of nesting in the definition, let’s give a recap of the properties of states in Setup
4
.

In Setup
4
the client holds a tuple of key pairs in register
{\boldsymbol{K}}=({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}
,
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{b})_{b\in\{0,1\}}
. And the client additionally holds a tuple of phase pairs in register
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}=({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}
,
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b})_{b\in\{0,1\}}
,
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
.
{\boldsymbol{H}}^{\prime}
is defined to be the blinded oracle where entries in the form of
\{0,1\}^{\kappa}||K^{({\text{switch}})}||\cdots
are blinded, which covers the target registers of
{\sf ReviseRO}
.
{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}
is defined to be the set of server-side operators that could only query this blinded oracle.

A state
\ket{\varphi}
in Setup
4
satisfies:

•

It is key checkable for each key pair in
{\boldsymbol{K}}
by an operator in
{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}
;

•

It is strongly-claw-free for each key pair in
{\boldsymbol{K}}
against efficient operators in
{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}
;

•

For each possible value
\Theta
of registers
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
, the corresponding component of
\ket{\varphi}
has norm
\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})|}}
.

•

For any
i\in[0,L],b\in\{0,1\}
, the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{1-b}
-branch of
\ket{\varphi}
does not depend on the value of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{b}
.

10
Analysis of Collective Phase Test (
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
)

In this section, we will analyze the implication of collective phase test.

Before going to the protocol analysis, in Section
10.1
we will define a series of notions including the
pre-phase-honest form
, and the
phase-honest form
.
Then in Section
10.2
, we will define a new randomization operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
.

•

Following the definition, we will show this operator will transform a state in Setup
4
(a basis-honest form with a specific property) to a pre-phase-honest form.

•

In Section
10.3
we will show if an efficient adversary could pass the collective phase test from a state in Setup
4
, the overall state could be further randomized under
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
.

10.1
Pre-phase-honest Form and Phase-honest Form

Let’s assume the Setup
4
and assume the state is in a basis-honest form for
{\boldsymbol{K}}
. We say this state is a pre-phase-honest form or a phase-honest form if it is a basis-honest form and has additional structure related to the phase information in
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
. Recall by Definition
6.7
and Notation
3.16
the basis-honest form could be written as a linear sum of different branches, and each branch could be written as the sum of different components based on different values of
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
register.

For preparation, let’s first define the
honest joint phase
of a branch:

Definition 10.1

(Honest joint phase)
.

Suppose the client holds a tuple of key pairs
{\boldsymbol{K}}=({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}
and a tuple of phase pairs
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}=({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(i)},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{1})
. For subscript vector
\vec{b}=b^{(0)}b^{(1)}b^{(2)}\cdots b^{(L)}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
, we call
{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})=\theta^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}+\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}+\theta^{(2)}_{b^{(2)}}+\cdots+\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}
the
honest joint phase
for
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch when the value of
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
is
\Theta
.

Then informally:

•

A pre-phase honest form satisfies: if
{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{1}})={\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{2}})
, then the
\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{1}}
branch is the same as the
\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{2}}
branch, excluding the registers that are necessarily different (which is the server-side key vector register
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}
and the client side phase register
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
). But we do not restrict the phases of states with different
{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})
.

•

A state in the phase-honest form means the branch
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
has phase
{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})
besides the requirement in pre-phase-honest form.

Definition 10.2

(Pre-phase-honest form)
.

We say a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
in Setup
4
is in the pre-phase honest form if there exists a family of states
\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},sum}}
for each
K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}),\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L},sum\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
such that
\ket{\varphi}
could be written as

\underbrace{\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\text{server-side ${\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}$}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(159)

Definition 10.3

(Phase-honest form)
.

We say a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
in Setup
4
is in the pre-phase honest form if there exists a family of states
\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},+}},\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},-}}
for each
K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}),\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
such that
\ket{\varphi}
could be written as

\underbrace{\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\text{server-side ${\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}$}}\otimes(e^{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},+}}+e^{-{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},-}})

(160)

As expected, in the formal definition we need to take the complex-conjugate term into consideration.

10.2
Randomization Operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}

Let’s start to define the randomization operator that randomizes a basis-honest form in Setup
4
to a pre-phase-honest form. To do that, we will define an operator
{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}
operated on the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch, for
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
.

Definition 10.4
.

Consider a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
in Setup
4
and a basis-honest form of
{\boldsymbol{K}}
:

\ket{\varphi}=\underbrace{\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\text{server-side ${\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}$}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,\vec{b}}}

Define the operator
{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}
controlled on a specific branch indexed by
\vec{b}=b^{(0)}b^{(1)}b^{(2)}\cdots b^{(L)}
, with randomness
\Delta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\Delta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}
:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{\Delta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\Delta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}}_{\text{client-side registers ${\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}$}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\text{server-side ${\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}$}}

(161)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}}

\displaystyle\ket{\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\underbrace{\ket{(\sum_{i\in[0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}-\sum_{i\in[L]}\Delta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}})\Delta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\Delta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}

(162)

and acts as identity on the other branches and outside
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}
.

This means:

•

This operator only operates on the
{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}}
-branch of the state, and randomizes the
\theta^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}
information stored on the client side with randomness
\Delta
. Recall that in the
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
protocol
\theta_{b^{(i)}}^{(i)}
could be decrypted with keys in
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
. Thus this operator aims at randomizing the phases that
could
be decrypted by the server’s keys, which is different from the operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
.

•

The randomization is done in a way that for each branch, the honest joint phase (Definition
10.1
) of this branch remains the same.

Then define the overall randomization operator for the collective phase test as the composition of
{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}
for each
\vec{b}
, with suitable choice of randomness:

Definition 10.5

(
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
)
.

For each
i\in[L]
,
b\in\{0,1\}
, initialize register
{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(i)}_{b}
to store a uniform superposition of
\{0,1\cdots 7\}
. Overall these registers are initialized to hold the state

\ket{\$_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8^{2L}}}\sum_{\forall b\in\{0,1\},i\in[L]:\Delta^{(i)}_{b}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{(\underbrace{\Delta_{b}^{(i)}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{b}^{(i)}})_{i\in[L],b\in\{0,1\}}}

(163)

Define
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
as

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}=\circ_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}

which operates on a state in Setup
4
together with
\ket{\$_{2}}
.

We have the following theorems about
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
.

Theorem 10.1
.

On state
\ket{\varphi}
in Setup
4
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
could be efficiently implemented with access to the transcript registers,
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}
and registers of
\ket{\$_{2}}
.

Proof.

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
can be implemented through the following operations.

1.

Use the key-checkable operators to calculate the subscript vector for keys in
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}
.

2.

For the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch, controlled by the subscript vector
\vec{b}
, apply operator
{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}
.

3.

Redo the first step to erase the temporary register that stores the subscripts.

∎

Theorem 10.2
.

If
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
4
and is in a basis-honest form,
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\varphi})
is in a pre-phase-honest form.

Proof.

By the definition of pre-phase-honest form we can study the structure of each branch separately. For
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
, denote the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch of
\ket{\varphi}
as

\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b}}}=\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\Theta,\vec{b}}}

where we make the client-side key registers implicit and make the phase registers explicit. By the condition that
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
4
, we know
\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b}}}
does not depend on the values of registers
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1-b^{(0)}}^{(0)}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1-b^{(1)}}^{(1)}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1-b^{(L)}}^{(L)}
. Thus we could write
\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b}}}
as:

\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b}}}=\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b},\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}\cdots\theta_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}}

By direct calculation, after the application of
{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}
it becomes (note that in the calculation below we omit some unused registers)

\displaystyle{\sf Add}_{\vec{b}}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b}}})

(164)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\forall i\in[L]:\Delta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\forall i\in[0,L]:\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\underbrace{\ket{(\sum_{i\in[0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}-\sum_{i\in[L]}\Delta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}})\Delta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\Delta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b},\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}\cdots\theta_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}}

(165)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\forall i\in[0,L]:\tilde{\Delta}^{(i)}_{b}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},\sum_{i\in[0,L]}\tilde{\Delta}^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}=\sum_{i\in 0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}}\ket{\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\ket{\tilde{\Delta}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}\tilde{\Delta}^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\tilde{\Delta}^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b},\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}\cdots\theta_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}}

(166)

which has the form required in the pre-phase-honest form if we define

\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b},sum}}=\sum_{\forall i\in[0,L]:\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},\sum_{i\in 0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}}=sum}\ket{\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta^{(1)}_{b^{(1)}}\cdots\theta^{(L)}_{b^{(L)}}}\ket{\varphi_{\vec{b},\theta_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}\theta_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}\cdots\theta_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}}

∎

10.3
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
Implies Approximate Invariance Under Randomization of
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}

Now we give the following theorem, which says the ability of passing
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
implies approximate invariance of the initial state under
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
.

Theorem 10.3
.

Suppose a sub-normalized purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
4
and is in
\epsilon_{0}
-basis-honest form. Suppose
{\mathsf{Adv}}
is an efficient adversary that could make the client output
{\mathsf{pass}}
in the collective phase test with probability
\geq 1-\epsilon_{1}
from initial state
\ket{\varphi}
. Then there is

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\varphi})\approx_{(12\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+\epsilon_{0}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\varphi}

(167)

10.3.1
A linear algebra lemma that connects state structure with randomization

Before giving the formal proof, we give a linear algebra lemma that connects the structure of states implies approximate invariance of an operation.

Fact 11
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}=\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{1+N}
. Suppose
\ket{\varphi}
,
\ket{\phi}
satisfy, there exist states
\ket{\varphi_{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}}
,
\ket{\phi_{sum}}
for each
c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
sum\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
such that

\ket{\varphi}=\sum_{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\ket{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}}

\ket{\phi}=\sum_{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}\in{\mathcal{C}}}\ket{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}\otimes\ket{\phi_{{\text{SUM}}(c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N})}}

\ket{\varphi}\approx_{\epsilon}\ket{\phi}

Then

\sum_{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\cdots\Delta_{N}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{N}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{8^{N}}}\ket{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\cdots\Delta_{N}}\otimes\ket{\varphi}

is
2\epsilon
-invariant under the following operator:

\ket{\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\cdots\Delta_{N}}\ket{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}\rightarrow\ket{c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}\ket{(\sum_{i\in[0,N]}c_{i}-\sum_{i\in[N]}\Delta_{i})\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\cdots\Delta_{N}}

(168)

Also as a preparation, we generalize Notation
3.17
a little bit:

Notation 10.1
.

We say a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
does not depend on the value of register
{\boldsymbol{C}}
for the same
f({\boldsymbol{C}})
if it can be written as

\ket{\varphi}=\sum_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}}\underbrace{\ket{c}}_{{\boldsymbol{C}}}\otimes\ket{\psi_{f(c)}}

10.3.2
Proof of Theorem
10.3

Proof.

Let’s use
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}
to denote the output state of running
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
on
\ket{\varphi}
against
{\mathsf{Adv}}
:

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}={\mathsf{CoPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}|^{2}\geq 1-\epsilon_{1}

(169)

The first step in the
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
protocol is a call to the
{\mathsf{Combine}}
protocol. This protocol combines
(1+L)
gadgets into one single gadget. Denote the output state after this step as
\ket{\varphi^{mid}}
:

\ket{\varphi^{mid}}:={\sf Calc}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)})\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket)

(170)

where

•

\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket
is the client-side messages in this step; recall that in this step the client samples
(r_{0}^{(i)},r_{1}^{(i)})
for each
i\in[L]
and prepares many look-up tables that encodes these
r
-values.

•

{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}
is the adversary’s operation in this step;

•

{\mathsf{Response}}
is the operation that the server sends back a response (which is the output of
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}
);

•

{\sf Calc}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)})
is the client-side operation that calculates
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)}
based on the server’s response.

Let’s first define some notations. In the passing space, suppose the server’s measurement outcome (in other words, the output of
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}
above) is

r_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}r_{b^{(2)}}^{(2)}\cdots r_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)},b^{(1)}\cdots b^{(L)}\in\{0,1\}^{L}

(171)

Recall the key vector notation in Notation
6.1
, when the key tuple is
K=(x_{0}^{(i)},x_{1}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})
and the phase tuple is
\Theta=(\theta_{0}^{(i)},\theta_{1}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})
, the final
K^{(combined)}
could be denoted as

K^{(combined)}=(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{1}})

where

\vec{b}_{0}=0b^{(1)}b^{(2)}\cdots b^{(L)},\vec{b}_{1}=1(1-b^{(1)})(1-b^{(2)})\cdots(1-b^{(L)})

(172)

Below we will use

\vec{b}_{0}+\vec{b}_{1}=\vec{1}\text{ (or $\vec{b}_{1}=\vec{1}-\vec{b}_{0}$)}

to denote
\vec{b}_{0},\vec{b}_{1}
that satisfy (
172
), and use
0||\{0,1\}^{L}
,
1||\{0,1\}^{L}
to denote the domain of
\vec{b}_{0},\vec{b}_{1}
above.

Correspondingly, the combined phases are

\Theta^{(combined)}=(\theta^{(combined)}_{0},\theta^{(combined)}_{1})=(\sum_{i\in[0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{b^{(i)}},\sum_{i\in[0,L]}\theta^{(i)}_{1-b^{(i)}})

Recall the notation of honest joint phase in Definition
10.1
, this could be written as

\Theta^{(combined)}=({\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{0}}),{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{1}}))

Now we analyze the protocol. Starting from
\ket{\varphi^{mid}}
, with
1/2
probability both parties will do a standard basis test on
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
. By (
169
) the passing probability of this step should be
\geq 1-2\epsilon_{1}
, by Theorem
6.2
we can expand the state
\ket{\varphi^{mid}}
based on the combined keys:

\exists\text{ efficient server-side }O:\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}:=O\ket{\varphi^{mid}},\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}\text{ is $1.5\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}$-basis-honest for ${\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}$}

(173)

We can assume the server-side register that holds one combined key is still
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}
. Then we could expand the basis-honest part of
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}
as follows:

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}

\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in 0||\{0,1\}^{L},\vec{b}_{1}\in 1||\{0,1\}^{L}:\vec{b}_{0}+\vec{b}_{1}=\vec{1}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}}\underbrace{\ket{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{0}}),{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{1}})}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{r_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}r_{b^{(2)}}^{(2)}\cdots r_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}}_{\text{transcript}}

\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{\vec{b}\in(\vec{b}_{0},\vec{b}_{1})}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,\vec{b}}}

To show (
167
), we will first show:

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}})\approx_{11\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}

(174)

Note that in the above expansion of
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}
we are considering the basis-honest form for the combined key, and there are only two branches corresponding to two keys in
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
. Denote the two branches as
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{mid}},\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{mid}}
:

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{0}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes

\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in 0||\{0,1\}^{L},\vec{b}_{1}\in 1||\{0,1\}^{L}:\vec{b}_{0}+\vec{b}_{1}=\vec{1}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}\ket{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{0}}),{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{1}})}\otimes\ket{r_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}r_{b^{(2)}}^{(2)}\cdots r_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b_{0}}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,\vec{b_{0}}}}

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{1}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes

\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in 0||\{0,1\}^{L},\vec{b}_{1}\in 1||\{0,1\}^{L}:\vec{b}_{0}+\vec{b}_{1}=\vec{1}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}\ket{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{0}}),{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}_{1}})}\otimes\ket{r_{b^{(1)}}^{(1)}r_{b^{(2)}}^{(2)}\cdots r_{b^{(L)}}^{(L)}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b_{1}}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,\vec{b_{1}}}}

Thus
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}=\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{0}}+\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{1}}
.

Then (
174
) is further reduced to proving:

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{0}})\approx_{5\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{0}}

(175)

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{1}})\approx_{5\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{1}}

(176)

Without loss of generality we prove (
175
). We first make use of the fact that the state and the adversary can also pass the Hadamard test (the other choice of the client in
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
). By (
169
) the passing probability of Hadamard test is
\geq 1-2\epsilon_{1}
.
By Lemma
6.4
we know
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}}
is claw-free for
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
. Then together with (
173
) by Corollary
7.3
there is:

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{mid}}

(177)

\displaystyle\approx_{2.5\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{mid}}.

(178)

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{mid}}

(179)

\displaystyle\approx_{2\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle-\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{mid}}.

(180)

Applying Fact

(with details in the box below) we know:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{mid}})

(181)

\displaystyle\approx_{2.5\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{mid}}.

(182)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{mid}})

(183)

\displaystyle\approx_{2\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle-\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\circ O^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{mid}}.

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{mid}_{1}}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\sum_{\vec{b}_{1}\in 1||\{0,1\}^{L}}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(combined)}}\circ O\circ{\sf Calc}\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}\circ\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in 0||\{0,1\}^{L}}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{0}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(combined)}}\circ O\circ{\sf Calc}\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}\circ\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{0}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\approx_{2.5\epsilon_{1}^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{b}_{1}\in 1||\{0,1\}^{L}}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(combined)}}\circ O\circ{\sf Calc}\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}\circ\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}_{1}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket)

(199)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}{\mathsf{Adv}}^{-1}_{1}{\mathsf{Response}}^{-1}\circ O^{-1}({\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes O\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}(\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}\rrbracket)))

where (

11
Analysis of the Individual Phase Test (
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
)

In this section we analyze the implication of the individual phase test (
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
).

1.

In Section
11.1
we will give two linear algebra lemmas which are the basis for later proofs.

2.

In Section
11.2
we show the optimal winning probability of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
is
OPT
. Recall that the
OPT
is defined to be
\frac{1}{3}\cos^{2}(\pi/8)
in Section
5
.

3.

In Section
11.3
we construct a randomization operator
{\mathcal{P}}
. We will show
{\mathcal{P}}
transforms a pre-phase-honest form to a phase-honest form.

4.

In Section
11.4
we show
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
implies approximate invariance of a state (under Setup
4
) under
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
.

We give a brief review and overview of our analysis of the
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
. Recall that in the
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
the client-side inputs are values of registers
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}
. Note that different values of
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}
corresponds to different server-side states. In more detail, when the value of
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}
is
(\theta^{(0)}_{0},\theta^{(0)}_{1})
, the malicious server’s state, assuming it’s in Setup
4
and basis-honest form of
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}
, could be expressed as (where we omit the client-side registers):

\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0}^{(0)}}}+\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}

Then
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
aims at testing the following property on
\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0}^{(0)}}}
,
\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}
: there exist states
\ket{\varphi_{0,+}},\ket{\varphi_{0,-}},\ket{\varphi_{1,+}},\ket{\varphi_{1,-}}
such that, up to a server-side isometry,

\text{for each $\theta_{0},\theta_{1}$, $b\in\{0,1\}$, }\ket{\varphi_{b,\theta_{b}}}\approx e^{\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{b,+}}+e^{-\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{b,-}}.

(206)

The first term corresponds to the honest state case and the second term corresponds to the complex-conjugated honest state case. Then starting from (
206
), we can show the state is approximate invariant under
{\mathcal{P}}
and thus close to a phase-honest form.

The analysis towards proving (
206
) roughly goes as follows. In
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
(Protocol
11
) both parties execute the Hadamard test with an extra phase bias. One of three choices of extra phase bias
\delta\in\{0,1,4\}
is chosen randomly, which corresponds to two cases:

•

\delta\in\{0,4\}
: the server is required to make the client output
{\mathsf{pass}}
in these two tests.

Considering the
\delta=0
case first. Suppose the adversary’s operation maps
\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta}}
to
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta}}
and maps
\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta}}
to
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta}}
. (Here
\theta
in
\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta}}
stands for
\theta_{0}^{(0)}
and
\theta
in
\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta}}
stands for
\theta_{1}^{(0)}
.) Then use
\Pi_{0}
to denote the projection onto the passing conditions (that is,
\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}
in Notation
7.1
). Then the server could pass the
\delta=0
case implies

\text{for each $\theta$, }\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta}}\approx\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta}}

(207)

Then in the
\delta=4
case the passing and failing conditions are (almost) opposite to each other, we can show

\text{for each $\theta$, }\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta}}\approx-\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta+4}}

(208)

(Recall that in the extra-phase-biased Hadamard test the adversary does not know
\delta
directly, it only knows
\theta_{1}^{(0)}-\theta_{0}^{(0)}-\delta
; thus if we fix the transcript and make
\delta
varies from
0
to
4
the client-side phase changes by
4
too.)

•

\delta=1
: the server is expected to make the client output
{\mathsf{win}}
to the score register with sufficiently high probability. As before we fix the transcript and the adversary’s operation is applied on a suitable superposition of
\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta}}
and
\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta+1}}
. Then could calculate the winning probability and get

\text{the winning probability is the sum of }|\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta}}+\Pi_{0}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta+1}}|^{2}\text{ for each $\theta$}

(209)

Then:

•

The optimality of
OPT
comes from bounding (
209
) under the conditions (
207
)(
208
) (and necessary properties on their norms). This is done in Section
11.2
.

•

When (
209
) is close to
OPT
, with this property together with (
207
)(
208
) we can prove these states satisfy some relations, which allow us to derive (
206
). This is done in Section
11.4
.

11.1
Linear Algebra Lemmas for Self-testing of State Sequences

In this subsection we prove two lemmas by linear algebra. These lemmas will be used in the analysis of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
.

First, we have the following lemma which leads to the optimality of
OPT
for winning probability:

Lemma 11.1
.

Suppose
\ket{\phi_{0,0}},\ket{\phi_{0,1}},\ket{\phi_{0,2}},\cdots,\ket{\phi_{0,7}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1,0}},\ket{\phi_{1,1}},\ket{\phi_{1,2}},\cdots,\ket{\phi_{1,7}}
satisfy the following for
\epsilon<0.001
:

•

There exist two non-negative real number
A_{0},A_{1}
such that
\forall i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
,
\frac{1}{8}A_{0}-\epsilon\leq|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}A_{0}
,
\frac{1}{8}A_{1}-\epsilon\leq|\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}A_{1}
,
A_{0}+A_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

•

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}-\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon

•

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}+\ket{\phi_{1,i+4}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon

Then

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}+\ket{\phi_{1,i+1}}|^{2}\leq\cos^{2}(\pi/8)+11\sqrt{\epsilon}

(210)

We also have the inverse version of this lemma, which characterize the self-testing property of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
. In more detail, if the left hand side of (
210
) is close-to-optimal, then these states should have a specific form, which is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 11.2
.

Suppose
\ket{\phi_{0,0}},\ket{\phi_{0,1}},\ket{\phi_{0,2}},\cdots,\ket{\phi_{0,7}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1,0}},\ket{\phi_{1,1}},\ket{\phi_{1,2}},\cdots,\ket{\phi_{1,7}}
satisfy the following for
\epsilon<10^{-5}
:

•

There exist two non-negative real number
A_{0},A_{1}
such that
\forall i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
,
\frac{1}{8}A_{0}-\epsilon\leq|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}A_{0}
,
\frac{1}{8}A_{1}-\epsilon\leq|\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}A_{1}
,
A_{0}+A_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

•

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}-\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon

•

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}+\ket{\phi_{1,i+4}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon

•

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i-1}}+\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\geq\cos^{2}(\pi/8)-\epsilon

Then define

\ket{\phi_{0,+}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,i}}

(211)

\ket{\phi_{0,-}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,i}}

(212)

\ket{\phi_{1,+}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{1,i}}

(213)

\ket{\phi_{1,-}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{1,i}}

(214)

there is

\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{b,i}}-(e^{i\cdot{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{b,+}}+e^{-i\cdot{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{b,-}})|^{2}\leq 640\epsilon^{1/4}

The proofs of Lemma
11.1
and
11.2
are given in Appendix
E
.

11.2
Optimality of
OPT
in
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}

In this section we prove the optimality of winning probability in the individual phase test.

Theorem 11.3

(Optimality of
OPT
in
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
)
.

Assume a sub-normalized purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
4
and is in
\epsilon_{0}
-basis-honest form for
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}
. Then for any
\epsilon_{1}<10^{-5}-2\epsilon_{0}
, any efficient adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
, at least one of the following two is true:

•

(Small passing probability)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\leq 1-\epsilon_{1}

•

(Small winning probability)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\leq OPT+10(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0})^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

Proof.

Expand
\ket{\varphi}
on the basis-honest form of
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}
:

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{\varphi}=\underbrace{\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\underbrace{\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,b}}

Define
\ket{\varphi_{0}}
,
\ket{\varphi_{1}}
as the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{1}
branches:

\ket{\varphi_{0}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,{0}}}

\ket{\varphi_{1}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,{1}}}.

Then
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{\varphi}=\ket{\varphi_{0}}+\ket{\varphi_{1}}
.

Define
\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}
as the component of
\ket{\varphi_{0}}
where the
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}
register is in value
\theta_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}
register is in value
\theta_{1}
:

\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta:{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}=\theta_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}=\theta_{1}}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,{0}}}

(215)

Similarly define the
\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}
:

\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta:{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}=\theta_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}=\theta_{1}}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,{1}}}

(216)

Then we have

\ket{\varphi_{0}}=\sum_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}},\ket{\varphi_{1}}=\sum_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}

Suppose

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}>1-\epsilon_{1}

(217)

Let’s calculate the probability of winning, which happens in the
\delta=1
case in the extra-phase-bias Hadamard test (Protocol
7
).

\displaystyle|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}

(218)

\displaystyle\approx_{2\epsilon_{0}}

\displaystyle|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{\varphi}|^{2}

(219)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})|^{2}

(220)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)},1;1^{\kappa})(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})|^{2}

(221)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)};1^{\kappa})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-1)|^{2}

(222)

Recall
\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}},\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}},\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}
are defined when we define the set-up of Hadamard test (Setup
2
, Notation
7.1
). In (
222
) we write explicitly the operations in which the client sends the relative phase with extra phase bias to the server, runs the protocol and projects onto the winning space.

Then we consider the
\delta=0
and
\delta=4
case in the
{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)},\delta)
subprotocol. By (
217
) the passing probability for these two cases should both be
\geq 1-3\epsilon_{1}
. We can do a similar calculation as (
218
)-(
222
) and get:

•

When
\delta=0
,

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)},0;1^{\kappa})\circ\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\geq 1-3\epsilon_{1}
. Recall the passing space is
\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}
. By Corollary
7.3
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)};1^{\kappa})(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}-\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0})|^{2}

(223)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(224)

•

When
\delta=4
, the passing space is
\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}
and
\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}
is in the failing space. Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)};1^{\kappa})(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-4)|^{2}

(225)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}

(226)

Define

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}=\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)};1^{\kappa})(\ket{\varphi_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}\odot\alpha)

(227)

Recall its subscripts correspond to the values of
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}
register and the transcript
\alpha
(
=\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-\delta
) of a specific branch.

Then define
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}
as the part of state in
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}
excluding the registers
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}
. That is,

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}=\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{0}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{1}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}

(228)

By the basis-phase correpondance property described in Setup
4
we know
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}
is the same for different
\theta_{1}
and
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}
is the same for different
\theta_{0}
. Thus we could introduce the following notation for these two states, where these unnecessary parameters are omitted:

\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1},\alpha}}

(229)

Then we can continue to calculate from (
222
):

(
222
)

(230)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-1}}+\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-1}}|^{2}

(231)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{3}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}}+\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{0}+1+\alpha,\alpha}}|^{2}

(232)

We will make use of Lemma
11.1
to bound the expression above. For the conditions to apply this lemma, we know:

\eqref{eq:171ma}\Rightarrow\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}-\ket{\psi_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(233)

\eqref{eq:173ma}\Rightarrow\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}+\ket{\psi_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}-4}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 3\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}

(234)

Finally by Lemma
7.2
we can argue about the norms of these states:

\forall b\in\{0,1\},\theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2},\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\alpha}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}|\ket{\varphi_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}|^{2}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)\leq\frac{1}{128}|\ket{\varphi_{b}}|^{2}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(235)

which together with (
233
)(
234
) allows us to apply Lemma
11.1
as follows. Define

\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}},

(236)

\ket{\psi_{1,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{0}+\alpha,\alpha}}

(237)

Then (
233
)(
234
)(
235
) translate to

\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}-\ket{\psi_{1,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(238)

\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}+\ket{\psi_{1,\theta_{0}+4}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(239)

\forall b\in\{0,1\},\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},|\ket{\psi_{b,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{16}|\ket{\varphi_{b}}|^{2}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(240)

then we get

(
232
)

(241)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0}}}+\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0}+1}}|^{2}

(242)

\displaystyle\text{(apply Lemma \ref{lem:optla}) }\leq

\displaystyle{\text{OPT}}+10(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{0})^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(243)

Substituting (
218
)(
219
) completes the proof.
∎

11.3
Randomization Operator
{\mathcal{P}}
for
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}

We will define the randomization operator
{\mathcal{P}}
for the
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
. Different from
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
, this operator will be a projection operator and will not use additional randomness. Let’s first give an intuitive discussion, and formalize it in Definition
11.1
.

11.3.1
Intuitive discussion

Let’s first consider the honest setting. The joint state of client side phase registers and the server-side state could be jointly written as

\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\frac{1}{8}\underbrace{\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}+e^{\theta_{1}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}})}_{\text{server}}

(244)

which is equal to the sum of two branches:

(\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\frac{1}{8}\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}}_{\text{server}})+(\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\frac{1}{8}\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}e^{\theta_{1}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}}_{\text{server}})

(245)

We will define two sub-operators
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+},{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}
that operate on the two branches correspondingly.
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
operates nontrivially only on the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}
-branch (the first term of (
245
)) while
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}
operates nontrivially only on the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(0)}
-branch (the second term of (
245
)). Without loss of generality, let’s show the design of
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
. As before, we will also see the honest input state is indeed invariant under this operator.

First note the first term of (
245
) could be seen as the following state on
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}
register tensoring other registers:

\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}

(246)

Introduce an indicator register
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
which hold
{\mathsf{false}}
value by default. Recall that
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}
are projections; we will use this indicator register to record whether these projections are successful (which means, if these projections are applied on honest inputs, the projections will always be successful, and this register will be flipped to
{\mathsf{true}}
deterministically). Then
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
applied on (
246
) goes as follows:

\displaystyle\text{Equation \eqref{eq:106}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}

(247)

(Control phase gate that adds phase
e^{-\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}
on
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}
register when it has value
\theta_{0}^{(0)}
)

(248)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\quad\text{(Note that it is $=\ket{+}\ket{+}\ket{+}$)}

(249)

(
{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}
, followed by a projection measurement on
\{\ket{0}\bra{0},\mathbb{I}-\ket{0}\bra{0}\}
; use
{\mathsf{true}}
to indicate
\ket{0}\bra{0}
and
{\mathsf{false}}
otherwise)

(250)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\ket{0}\ket{0}\ket{0}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}

(251)

(Reverse the Hadamard and phase operations)

(252)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{Equation \eqref{eq:106}}\otimes\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}

(253)

Besides
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+},{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}
, we also need to define
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-},{\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}
, as follows. As discussed in Section
2.1
, in the malicious setting there is no way so far to rule out the
complex conjugate attack
. Correspondingly, we define the sub-operators
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-},{\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}
that fix the complex conjugate of the honest state, which is

\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\frac{1}{8}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(e^{-\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}+e^{-\theta_{1}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}})}_{\text{server}}

(254)

The construction is similar to (
247
)-(
253
) with the following differences:

•

Compared to (
248
) the phase is changed to
e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}
.

•

The measurement results are stored in a different indicator register denoted by
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
.

Importantly, our construction of
{\mathcal{P}}_{b,+}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{b,-}
,
b\in\{0,1\}
have the following properties, which can be verified by a direct calculation:

•

When
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}
are applied on (
254
), the outcome value in the indicator register
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
is deterministically
{\mathsf{false}}
. When they are applied on (
244
)
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
is deterministically
{\mathsf{true}}
.

•

When
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}
is applied on (
244
), the outcome value in the indicator register
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
is deterministically
{\mathsf{false}}
. When they are applied on (
254
)
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
is deterministically
{\mathsf{true}}
.

Finally define
{\mathcal{P}}
to be the sequential application of each of these four suboperators with suitable projections. (The constructions guarantee that the order of these suboperators does not really matter as long as the initial state is in some specific form). And the outcome of the indicator registers will indicate whether the server-side state has honest phases or conjugated phases.

Below we give the formal definitions.

11.3.2
Formalization

Definition 11.1
.

Consider the register setup in Section
6.1
. Explicitly, we can assume a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
where the operators will act nontrivially on is in the basis-honest form of key pair
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}
(since our operators will act as identity on spaces outside this form):

\ket{\varphi}=\underbrace{\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\sum_{\Theta^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)})}\ket{\Theta^{(0)}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}^{(0)}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\Theta,b}}

First define some intermediate operators that will be used in our construction.

•

Define the control-phase operator that controlled on the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{b}
-branch, adds a phase determined by
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{b}^{(0)}
:

{\sf CPhase}_{b}(+):\underbrace{\ket{(x_{0}^{(0)},x_{1}^{(0)})}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{b}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b}}\underbrace{\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}^{(0)}_{bsh}}\rightarrow\ket{(x_{0}^{(0)},x_{1}^{(0)})}e^{\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\theta_{b}^{(0)}}\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}

{\sf CPhase}_{b}(-):\ket{(x_{0}^{(0)},x_{1}^{(0)})}\ket{\theta_{b}^{(0)}}\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}\rightarrow\ket{(x_{0}^{(0)},x_{1}^{(0)})}e^{-\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\theta_{b}^{(0)}}\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}

•

Define
{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b},{\boldsymbol{indic}}}
as the control-flip operator that flip the state of
{\boldsymbol{indic}}
when
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{b}
register is in all-zero state.

•

We use
{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{b}^{(0)}}
to denote the bit-wise Hadamard on client-side register
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{b}^{(0)}
.

Initialize single-bit registers
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
to hold
{\mathsf{false}}
by default.
Define suboperators as follows:

{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}={\sf CPhase}_{0}(+){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}{\sf CPhase}_{0}(-)

{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}={\sf CPhase}_{0}(-){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}{\sf CPhase}_{0}(+)

{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}={\sf CPhase}_{1}(+){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}}{\sf CPhase}_{1}(-)

{\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}={\sf CPhase}_{1}(-){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1}}{\sf CPhase}_{1}(+)

Finally the overall randomization operator is:

{\mathcal{P}}=(\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}(\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}+\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}})+\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(0)}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}(\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}+\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}})){\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}

(255)

Recall
\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{b}^{(0)}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}
is the projection onto the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{b}^{(0)}
-branch of the basis-honest part.

We add some comments for understanding this definition. First note
{\mathcal{P}}_{1,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{1,+}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
are all defined to be unitary operators here and projections happen in (
255
). Note multiplication of commuting projectors is equivalent to logical and and summation of orthogonal projectors is equivalent to logical or. If we focus on each single term of (
255
), and ignore the possible interference between different
{\mathcal{P}}_{b,\pm}
, this construction is the same as what we intuitively discussed in Section
11.3.1
. In the next subsubsection we will formally analyze how the honest state, or its complex conjugate, evolves under this operator.

11.3.3
{\mathcal{P}}
behaves well on states with honest phases or its complex conjugates

As discussed in the beginning of Section
11
, we aim at testing (
206
). Here we first prove the form of states shown in the right hand side of (
206
) do behave well under
{\mathcal{P}}
. In more detail,
{\mathcal{P}}
will revise the indicator registers
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
,
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
; if the phase is
e^{\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}
as shown in the first term in (
206
) the
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
register will be flipped to
{\mathsf{true}}
, and if the phase is
e^{-\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}
as shown in the second term in (
206
) the
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
register will be flipped to
{\mathsf{true}}
. In addition, when the initial state is in the form of (
206
),
{\mathcal{P}}
behaves as a unitary; thus
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
maps the state to the original state, which is similar to the case where
{\mathcal{R}}_{1},{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
are applied on corresponding honest states.

Below we formalize this discussion as a lemma.

Lemma 11.4
.

Suppose the register setup is the same as Section
6.1
, especially, the client holds key pair
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}=({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(0)})
and the corresponding phase pair
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}=({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{1})
. Suppose the purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
satisfies: for each
b\in\{0,1\},K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})
there exist states
\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},b,+}}
,
\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},b,-}}
such that:

\ket{\varphi}=\underbrace{\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\sum_{\Theta^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)})}\ket{\Theta^{(0)}}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\underbrace{\ket{x_{b}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}\otimes(e^{\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},b,+}}+e^{-\theta_{b}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},b,-}})

Then
{\mathcal{P}}\ket{\varphi}
is the linear sum of the following states:

\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\otimes\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{\theta^{(0)}_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},0,+}}

(256)

\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\otimes\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{-\theta^{(0)}_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},0,-}}

(257)

\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\otimes\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{\theta^{(0)}_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},1,+}}

\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\otimes\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes\ket{x_{1}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{-\theta^{(0)}_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},1,-}}

And

{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}\ket{\varphi}=\ket{\varphi}

(258)

Proof.

The proof is by a direct calculation as discussed before this theorem. Without loss of generality we calculate on the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}
branch. Then we only need to prove

\displaystyle(\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}+\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}){\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\sum_{K^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{K^{(0)}}\otimes\sum_{\Theta^{(0)}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)})}\ket{\Theta^{(0)}}\otimes

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ket{x_{0}^{(0)}}\otimes(e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},0,+}}+e^{-\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\varphi_{K^{(0)},0,-}})

(259)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:118}+\eqref{eq:119}

(260)

which is further reduced to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}

(261)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}e^{\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}

(262)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}e^{-\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}

(263)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}e^{-\theta_{0}^{(0)}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\sum_{\theta_{0}^{(0)},\theta_{1}^{(0)}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}\ket{\theta_{0}^{(0)}}\ket{\theta_{1}^{(0)}}

(264)

which are true by direct calculations as (
247
)-(
253
). (As an example, in (
261
) the application of
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
maps the state to (
262
), and then
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
will keep the state invariant since the first Hadamard transform in
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
maps the
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}
register to
\ket{110}
on which
{\sf M}
operator acts as identity.)

Then the projections in the definition of
{\mathcal{P}}
acts as identity thus (
258
) follows.
∎

11.3.4
{\mathcal{P}}
projects a pre-phase-honest form to a phase-honest form

The restriction so far is, we are only focusing on a single pair of phases
\Theta^{(0)}
, which are the phases got tested in
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
.
{\mathcal{P}}
only operates on a single phase pair, but we want to argue about the overall property of the whole state—that is, the overall state should be in the phase-honest form (Definition
10.3
). The next lemma says, when the input state is a pre-phase-honest form, the output of
{\mathcal{P}}
will be a phase-honest form:

Lemma 11.5
.

Suppose the register setup is the same as Section
6.1
, especially, the client holds a tuple of key pairs
{\boldsymbol{K}}=({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}
,
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(i)}_{1})
, and a tuple of phase pairs
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}=({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)})_{i\in[0,L]}
,
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(i)}_{1})
. Suppose a purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
is in the pre-phase-honest form. Then there exist states
\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},+}}
,
\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},-}}
, (for each
K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})
,
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
) such that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}\ket{\varphi}

(265)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes e^{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},+}}

(266)

\displaystyle+\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\otimes e^{-{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},-}}

(267)

Proof.

Since
\ket{\varphi}
is in a pre-phase-honest form, we can assume there exist states
\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},sum}}
(for each
K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})
,
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
,
sum\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
) such that
\ket{\varphi}
has the form

\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(268)

Without loss of generality consider
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{1+L}
whose first bit is
0
. Then from the definition of
{\mathcal{P}}
we have two terms to calculate. Let’s first calculate
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}
. First we know:

\displaystyle\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}

(269)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf CPhase}_{0}(+){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}{\sf CPhase}_{0}(-)\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(270)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf CPhase}_{0}(+){\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}{\sf H}^{\otimes 3}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes e^{-\theta^{(0)}_{0}\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(271)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf CPhase}_{0}(+)\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\alpha\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},\alpha-\theta^{(0)}_{0}+{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(272)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-(\alpha-\theta^{(0)}_{0})\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},\alpha-\theta^{(0)}_{0}+{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}})}}

(273)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes e^{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\beta\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},\beta}}

(274)

Then as shown in Lemma
11.4

{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
keeps (
274
) invariant. Thus we get

\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}=\eqref{eq:220}

which has the form of state required in (
266
) if we define

\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},+}}:=\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\otimes\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\beta\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},\beta}}

Now we calculate
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}
and show it has the form required in (
267
). We could do a similar direct calculation; the case here is slightly more complicated (note that
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
are not known to be commutative) but still possible; but here we choose a short path where we re-use the calculations we did just now.

Recall the construction of
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
, it has the form of
U^{\dagger}{\sf M}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(0)}_{0},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}U
. That implies when the initial state has value
\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}
in
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
,
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
is the same as
\mathbb{I}-{\sf X}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}
, where
{\sf X}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}
is an operator that flips the value of register
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}
. Thus

\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}=\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}-{\sf X}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}=\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\vec{b}}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\varphi}-{\sf X}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\eqref{eq:220}

Note the second term is invariant under
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
thus satisfies
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}\eqref{eq:220}=0
. This implies

\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\ket{\varphi}=\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}\ket{\varphi}

Then a calculation similar to (
269
) to (
274
) shows this term has the form of (
267
) if we define

\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},-}}:=\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\otimes\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\beta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\beta\pi{\mathrm{i}}/4}\ket{\varphi_{K,\vec{b},\beta}}

∎

11.4
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
Implies Approximate Invariance Under
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}

In this section we show passing
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
implies approximate invariance under
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
.

Theorem 11.6
.

Suppose a sub-normalized purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
4
and is in a
\epsilon
-basis-honest form. Suppose an efficient adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
on initial state
\ket{\varphi}
in
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
can make the client output
{\mathsf{pass}}
as the flag with probability
\geq 1-\epsilon
and make the client output
{\mathsf{win}}
as the score with probability
\geq{\text{OPT}}-\epsilon
. Then we have

|(\mathbb{I}-{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}})\ket{\varphi}|\leq 50\epsilon^{1/16}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

Proof.

First we use a similar argument to the proof of Theorem
11.3
. Similarly define
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0}}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0}}}
as (
236
)(
237
). As given in the condition, suppose an adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
can pass the individual phase test with high probability:

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\geq 1-\epsilon

By the same argument we know

\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}-\ket{\psi_{1,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}+\ket{\psi_{1,\theta_{0}+4}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

\forall b\in\{0,1\},\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},|\ket{\psi_{b,\theta_{0}}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{16}|\ket{\varphi_{b}}|^{2}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

where
|\ket{\varphi_{b}}|
is the norm of
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{b}^{(0)}
-branch of
\ket{\varphi}
.

If the server can win with significant probability:

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\geq{\text{OPT}}-\epsilon

\Rightarrow|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\geq{\text{OPT}}-3\epsilon

As in the proof of Theorem
11.3
, it implies

\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0}}}+\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0}+1}}|^{2}\geq\cos^{2}(\pi/8)-9\epsilon

Now applying Lemma
11.2
we know

\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0}}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,+}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,-}})|^{2}\leq 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(275)

where

\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,+}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,-}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\theta}}

(276)

Recall the definition of
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0}}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0}}}
in (
236
)(
237
). Unroll (
275
) by substituting definitions (
236
)(
237
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,+}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,-}})|^{2}

(277)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{0}+\alpha,\alpha}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,+}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,-}})|^{2}

(278)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(279)

Recall in the definition of
