\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0}}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0}}}
,
\alpha
is store in a separate transcript register thus
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}}
(and also
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{0}+\alpha,\alpha}}
) for different
\alpha
are orthogonal states. Thus we can expand the norm-square-of-sum in (
277
) to sum-of-norm-square on values of
\alpha
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\alpha}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,+,\alpha}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,-,\alpha}})|^{2}

(280)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{0}+\alpha,\alpha}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,+,\alpha}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,-,\alpha}})|^{2}

(281)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(282)

where
\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{b,\pm,\alpha}}
are defined to be the corresponding component of (
276
). Explicitly, they are:

\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,+,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta,\cdot,\alpha}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,-,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta,\cdot,\alpha}}

(283)

\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,+,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta+\alpha,\alpha}},\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,-,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta+\alpha,\alpha}}

(284)

So far we are working on variables
\theta_{0}
and
\alpha
, which corresponds to the value of client-side register
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}
and client’s message in the Hadamard test. We use a change-of-variable to introduce
\theta_{1}
to replace
\alpha
. First define

\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,+,\alpha}},\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,-,\alpha}}\text{ the same as \eqref{eq:274qa}}

\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,+,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta,\alpha}},\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,-,\alpha}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta,\alpha}}

Then (
280
)-(
282
) could be re-written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}

(285)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-(e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}

(286)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(287)

As discussed in the paragraph above (
229
), the “
\cdot
” could be replaced by any value without changing the state. This implies (
285
)-(
287
) could be further re-written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}

(288)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-(e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}

(289)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(290)

This can be further unrolled by (
228
):

\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-\underbrace{\ket{\theta_{0}}\ket{\theta_{1}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}}\otimes(e^{\theta_{b}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{b,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{b}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{b,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}\leq 1200\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(291)

Recall (
223
) translates to

\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}-\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}|^{2}\leq 6\sqrt{2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

which together with (
291
) implies

\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

-\ket{\theta_{0}}\ket{\theta_{1}}\otimes(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}})|^{2}\leq 2450\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(292)

Recall

\displaystyle\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

(293)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\delta=0)(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})

(294)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{3\epsilon}}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\delta=0)(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})

(295)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}((\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}))

(296)

And we have

\displaystyle e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,+,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

(297)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta,\cdot,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\cdot,\theta,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

(298)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{0,\theta+\theta_{0},\theta+\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi^{\prime}_{1,\theta+\theta_{0},\theta+\theta_{1},\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

(299)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}(\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\delta=0)e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0}+\theta,\theta_{1}+\theta}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0}+\theta,\theta_{1}+\theta}})

(300)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{3\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}({\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}\circ e^{-\theta{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}(\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0}+\theta,\theta_{1}+\theta}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0}+\theta,\theta_{1}+\theta}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}))

(301)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}((e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,+}}+e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,+}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}))

(302)

where

\ket{\psi_{0,+}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot}},\ket{\psi_{1,+}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1}}}

where
\ket{\psi_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}
is defined by the
{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(0)}=\theta_{0},\theta_{1}
component of
\ket{\varphi_{b}}
excluding the
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{1}
registers. (That is,
\ket{\varphi_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}=\ket{\theta_{0}}\ket{\theta_{1}}\ket{\psi_{b,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}
.), and by the basis-phase correspondence property a subscript could be omitted.

Similarly

\displaystyle e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{0,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1,-,\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}}}

(303)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{3\epsilon}}

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}((e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,-}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,-}})\odot(\theta_{1}-\theta_{0}))

(304)

where

\ket{\psi_{0,-}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta_{0}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,\theta_{0},\cdot}},\ket{\psi_{1,-}}=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,\cdot,\theta_{1}}}

Substitute these approximations and reverse
{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}
from (
292
) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\theta_{0},\theta_{1}\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}^{2}}|\ket{\varphi_{0,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,\theta_{0},\theta_{1}}}-\ket{\theta_{0}}\ket{\theta_{1}}\otimes(e^{\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,+}}+e^{\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,+}}+e^{-\theta_{0}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{0,-}}+e^{-\theta_{1}{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\psi_{1,-}})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2500\epsilon^{1/8}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(305)

Thus (
305
) says
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)})}\ket{\varphi}
is close to a state in the form of (
206
). By Lemma
11.4
a state in the form of (
206
) is invariant under
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
. This implies

|(\mathbb{I}-{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}})\ket{\varphi}|\leq 50\epsilon^{1/16}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

∎

12
Analysis of the basis uniformity test (
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
)

In this section we analyze the implication of passing the basis uniformity test (
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
) with high probability. As discussed in the introduction, it implies different standard basis components of the initial state have approximately equal norms.

12.1
Initial Setup of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}

Recall in the formal protocol
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
is defined in two steps:
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}})
is applied on the output state of
{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}
, and in this protocol the client reveals
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
to the server and allows it to remove the phases; then both parties run
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})
. In the next subsection we will analyze the property of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})
. (Recall that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}
denotes
({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)})_{i\in[L]}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
denotes
({\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{(i)})_{i\in[L]}
; see Section
6.1
.)

The initial state of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}})
is in Setup
3
, while the initial state of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})
does not have a corresponding setup that describes it. Below we formalize the properties of input states of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})
as a setup.

Set-up 5
.

Setup
5
is defined to be the set of states that satisfy:

•

The parties are as described in Section
4.1
.

•

The client holds a tuple of key pair registers
\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}={\boldsymbol{K}}^{(1)},{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(2)}\cdots{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(L)}
, where each
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}=({\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(i)},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(i)})
. Each key has length
\kappa
. Correspondingly the server holds registers
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(1)},{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(2)}\cdots{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(L)}
, where each register has size
\kappa
. Denote
\tilde{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}
as the tuple of registers
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(1)},{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(2)}\cdots{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(L)}
.

•

The state is efficiently preparable;

•

The state is key checkable for any key in
\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}
;

•

The state is strongly-claw-free for any key pair in
\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}
.

Lemma 12.1
.

If a sub-normalized state
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
3
,
\ket{\varphi}\odot\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}
is in Setup
5
.

Proof.

By the condition we know
\ket{\varphi}={\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}}),{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{1}}
where
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
is in Setup
1
. By the definition of Setup
1

\ket{\varphi^{1}}
is strongly-claw-free for any key pair in
({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}})
. Then the claw-freeness of
\ket{\varphi}
follows from Lemma
6.4
.
∎

Below we give the theorem for
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
.

12.2
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
Implies Basis Norms Are Close to Uniform Vectors

Theorem 12.2

(Implication of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
)
.

Suppose a sub-normalized purified joint state
\ket{\varphi}
is in Setup
5
and is in a basis-honest form.
Suppose
{\mathsf{Adv}}
is an efficient adversary such that

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}|^{2}\leq\epsilon

(306)

Expand the basis-honest part of
\ket{\varphi}
:

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\varphi}=\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\underbrace{\ket{\tilde{K}}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},\vec{b}}}

(307)

and define the norm of
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch as
c_{\vec{b}}
:

c_{\vec{b}}:=|\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},\vec{b}}}|

Additionally define

c:=|\ket{\varphi}|

Then we have

\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c_{\vec{b}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{L}}}c|^{2}\leq 800\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

Note that when we apply this theorem the initial state
\ket{\varphi}
might be far from normalized. Due to this reason, we use the failing probability in (
306
) instead of the passing probability, which makes the expression simpler and easier to use later.

Proof.

The
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
will first combine the state into a two-key superposition state on an index set
I=(i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{|I|})\subseteq[L]
randomly selected by the client. In this process (see Protocol
9
) the server will send back
|I|-1
output strings denoted by symbol
r
in the protocol, and these output strings, in the passing space, will help the client determine
K^{(combined)}
as follows:

K^{(combined)}=(x^{(combined)}_{0},x^{(combined)}_{1})=(x_{0}^{(i_{1})}x_{b^{(i_{2})}}^{(i_{2})}\cdots x_{b^{(i_{|I|})}}^{(i_{|I|})},x_{1}^{(i_{1})}x_{1-b^{(i_{2})}}^{(i_{2})}\cdots x_{1-b^{(i_{|I|})}}^{(i_{|I|})}),\quad b^{(i_{2})}b^{(i_{3})}\cdots b^{(i_{|I|})}\in\{0,1\}^{|I|-1}

(308)

where the values of these subscripts (that is,
b
) are determined by the server’s response (that is,
r
.)

Denote the output state of the first step (the key combination) of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
as
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
.

Then starting from
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
, the server is supposed to measure all the keys with superscripts in
[L]-I
in the standard basis and send back the result. Denote
[L]-I=j_{1}j_{2}\cdots j_{L-|I|}
. The server’s response, on the passing space, could be expressed as

x^{(j_{1})}_{b^{(j_{1})}}x^{(j_{2})}_{b^{(j_{2})}}\cdots x^{(j_{L-|I|})}_{b^{(j_{L-|I|})}},\quad b^{(j_{1})}\cdots b^{(j_{L-|I|})}\in\{0,1\}^{L-|I|}

(309)

Denote the output state after this step as
\ket{\varphi^{2}}
.

Let’s introduce notations that describe (
308
)(
309
) more concisely, with
I=(i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{|I|})\subseteq[L]
and
\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}
, where the
i_{1}
-th bit of
\vec{b}_{0}
is
0
.

Define
X{(I)}\vec{b}_{0}
as the output vector of doing a logical-not on each bit of
\vec{b}_{0}
whose index is in
I
. And define

\vec{b}_{0}|_{I}=b^{(i_{1})}b^{(i_{2})}b^{(i_{3})}\cdots b^{(i_{|I|})},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I}=x_{b^{(i_{1})}}^{(i_{1})}x_{b^{(i_{2})}}^{(i_{2})}\cdots x_{b^{(i_{|I|})}}^{(i_{|I|})}

\vec{b}_{0}|_{[L]-I}=b^{(j_{1})}b^{(j_{2})}\cdots b^{(j_{L-|I|})},\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}=x^{(j_{1})}_{b^{(j_{1})}}x^{(j_{2})}_{b^{(j_{2})}}\cdots x^{(j_{L-|I|})}_{b^{(j_{L-|I|})}}

Then (
308
)(
309
) could be expressed as follows.

\eqref{eq:84bn}=(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I}),\eqref{eq:85bn}=\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}.

(310)

Then
\ket{\varphi^{2}}
will go to the standard basis test with
1/2
probability, where the server is suppose to measure the combined keys and return a response in
K^{(combined)}
.
By (
306
) the adversary could fail the standard basis test with probability
\leq 2\epsilon
, by Theorem
6.2
we know there exists an efficient server-side isometry
\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}
such that, define

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}:=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}\ket{\varphi^{2}}

then

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
is
1.5\sqrt{\epsilon}
-basis-honest for
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
.

(311)

Then
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
has the following form:

\underbrace{\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})}\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}:\text{the $i_{1}$-th bit is $0$, where $i_{1}$ is the first bit of $I$}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I})}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}}}_{\text{client}}

\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}}}_{\text{server's response \eqref{eq:85bn}}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{\vec{b}_{0},X(I)\vec{b}_{0}\}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}|I}}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},I,\vec{b}}}

where we use the notations in (
310
).

Define
c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}}
as the norm of the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch of
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
when
{\boldsymbol{I}}
has value
I
,
which is,

|\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I})}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}|[L]-I}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}|I}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},I,\vec{b}}}|.

(312)

where in (
312
)
\vec{b}_{0}
is defined to be
\vec{b}
if the
i_{1}
-th bit of
\vec{b}
is
0
, and
X(I)\vec{b}
otherwise.

We will first prove
(c_{\vec{b}})_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}
and
(c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}})_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}
are close to each other. These two vectors are not directly comparable since we need to define
c_{I,\vec{b}}
for different values of register
{\boldsymbol{I}}
. That is, starting from (
307
), after the client samples random values for the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register, the state on the basis-honest space becomes:

\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})}\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})|}}\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},\vec{b}}}

Then we can define
c_{I,\vec{b}}
as the norm of the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch when the value of
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register is
I
:

c_{I,\vec{b}}=|\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})|}}\ket{\varphi_{\tilde{K},\vec{b}}}|\quad(\text{which is $=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{L}}}c_{\vec{b}}$})

(313)

By making use of Lemma
6.6
we can prove

\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}}),\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c_{I,\vec{b}}-c_{I,\vec{b}}^{\prime}|^{2}\leq 3\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(314)

The proof details are put in the box below for continuity of proof stream.

Proof of (
314
):

Consider the mapping between
\ket{\varphi}
and
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
.

The client-side message in the first round of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
could be denoted as
\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket
. Let
D=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}\circ{\mathsf{Response}}\circ{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}
where
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{1}
is the adversary’s operation in the first round, and
{\mathsf{Response}}
is the adversary’s measure-and-response operation in the first and second round. By the definition of
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
:

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}={\sf Calc}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})\circ D(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket)

where we use
{\sf Calc}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})
to denote the client-side operation that calculates
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
from the values of server’s response
r
.

By the basis-honest property of
\ket{\varphi}
and
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
we have

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}{\sf Calc}^{-1}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}D\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket)

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}D^{-1}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}{\sf Calc}^{-1}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}

Apply Lemma
6.6
to the right hand sides of both equations, we get

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}D\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket)

(315)

And

\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{Combine}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{I}};1^{\kappa})\rrbracket\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}}D^{-1}\Pi_{\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}

(316)

Compare the amplitudes on both sides.

•

In the left hand side of (
315
) the amplitude of
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch when the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register has value
I
is
c_{I,\vec{b}}
, while on the right hand side the amplitude of
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch when the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register has value
I
is no more than
c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}}
.

•

Similarly for (
316
) in the left hand side the amplitude of
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch when the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register has value
I
is
c_{I,\vec{b}}
, while on the right hand side the amplitude of
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
branch when the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register has value
I
is no more than
c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}}
.

Applying Fact
4
completes the proof.

And from
\ket{\varphi^{2}}
with
1/2
probability the state comes into a Hadamard test. By (
306
) we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}|^{2}\leq 2\epsilon

(317)

where we use
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}
to denote the part of
{\mathsf{Adv}}
’s operation in the Hadamard test branch of the third step of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
. Then there is (whose proof is put into a box below for the continuity of the proof stream)

\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}}),\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}}-c^{\prime}_{I,X{(I)}\vec{b}}|^{2}\leq 170\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(318)

Proof of (
318
):

Define
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}_{0}}
,
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}_{1}}
as correspondingly the
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(combined)}_{0}
,
{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(combined)}_{1}
branches of
\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2}}
. That is,

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{2}}=\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})}\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}:\text{the $i_{1}$-th bit is $0$, where $i_{1}$ is the first bit of $I$}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I})}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}}

\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}}}_{\text{server's response \eqref{eq:85bn}}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{I,\tilde{K},\vec{b}_{0},0}}

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{2}}=\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}})}\ket{I}\otimes\sum_{\tilde{K}\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{K}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}:\text{the $i_{1}$-th bit is $0$, where $i_{1}$ is the first bit of $I$}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I})}}_{{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}}

\otimes\underbrace{\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}}}_{\text{server's response \eqref{eq:85bn}}}\otimes\ket{\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I}}\otimes\ket{\varphi_{I,\tilde{K},\vec{b}_{0},1}}

We will show (
317
) implies an efficient server-side operator
O
that

O(\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{2}}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}\rrbracket\odot{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)})\approx_{9\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{2}}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}\rrbracket\odot{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}

(319)

(where
\llbracket{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}\rrbracket
contains the random padding stored in register
{\boldsymbol{pad}}
).
O
is constructed as follows:

1.

Apply
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}
(but do not do
{\mathsf{Response}}
). Recall in the real execution of the protocol
{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}
after the server’s local operation a
{\mathsf{Response}}
operator copies the value of some server-side register to the transcript register
{\boldsymbol{d}}
. Denote this server-side register that stores the (unsent) response as
\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}
.

2.

Do a control phase operation on the
\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}
register that adds a
(-1)
phase on the basis value
d\in{\text{Domain}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}})
such that

d\cdot(x_{0}^{(combined)}||H(pad||x_{0}^{(combined)}))+d\cdot(x_{1}^{(combined)}||H(pad||x_{1}^{(combined)}))=1

(that is, the space
{\text{span}}(\Pi^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1})
).

3.

Reverse
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}
.

Let’s show (
319
). This is because, under the condition of (
317
), applying Corollary
7.3
we get

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{2}}

(320)

\displaystyle\approx_{3\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{2}}

(321)

\forall b\in\{0,1\},\Pi_{=0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{b}}\approx_{2\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}0

(322)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{2}}

(323)

\displaystyle\approx_{2\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle-\Pi^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }\tilde{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{HT}{\mathsf{Adv}}_{ST}^{\dagger}}({\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{1}^{2}}

(324)

Combining these relations with the construction of
O
completes the proof of (
319
).

Now return to the proof of (
318
). We compare the coefficients on both sides of (
319
). On the right hand side of (
319
) the norm of components where the
{\boldsymbol{I}}
register has value
I
,
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(combined)}
register has value
(\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|I},\vec{x}_{X(I)\vec{b}_{0}|I})
, and the server-response register has value
\vec{x}_{\vec{b}_{0}|[L]-I}
, is
c^{\prime}_{I,X(I)\vec{b}_{0}}
. On the left hand side this norm is
c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}_{0}}
(note that
O
only has read-only access to the registers above thus it does not change the norm). Thus (
319
) implies

\sqrt{\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}}),\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}:\text{the $i_{1}$-th bit of $\vec{b}_{0}$ is $0$, where $i_{1}$ is the first bit of $I$}}|c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}_{0}}-c^{\prime}_{I,X{(I)}\vec{b}_{0}}|^{2}}\leq 9\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(325)

which by a change of variable implies

\sqrt{\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}}),\vec{b}_{1}\in\{0,1\}^{L}:\text{the $i_{1}$-th bit of $\vec{b}_{1}$ is $1$, where $i_{1}$ is the first bit of $I$}}|c^{\prime}_{I,X{(I)}\vec{b}_{1}}-c^{\prime}_{I,\vec{b}_{1}}|^{2}}\leq 9\epsilon^{1/4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(326)

which together implies (
318
).

Combining (
314
)(
318
) we get

\sum_{I\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{I}}),\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c_{I,\vec{b}}-c_{I,X{(I)}\vec{b}}|^{2}\leq 180\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(327)

then substitute (
313
) we know
(
327
) implies

\frac{1}{2^{L}}\sum_{\vec{b}_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\sum_{\vec{b}_{1}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c_{\vec{b}_{0}}-c_{\vec{b}_{1}}|^{2}\leq 180\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

which by Fact
5
implies

\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}|c_{\vec{b}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{L}}}c|^{2}\leq 800\sqrt{\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

∎

13
Putting All Together

In this section we put the analysis of each subprotocol together. We prove the optimality of
OPT
in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
in Section
13.1
and prove the verifiability property of
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
in Section
13.2
. (Recall these statements are formalized in Section
5.4
). Recall that

{\text{OPT}}=\frac{1}{3}\cos^{2}(\pi/8),p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}=\frac{1}{10},p_{{\mathsf{comp}}}=\frac{1}{10}

(328)

13.1
Proof of the Optimality of
OPT

To prove the optimality of
OPT
, we need to analyze each step of Protocol
2
, and make use of the property of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Suppose the initial purified joint state is
\ket{\varphi^{0}}=O\ket{0}
as described in Definition
4.6
. Suppose an efficient adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
satisfies

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{0}}|>1-10^{-2000}

(329)

1.

(Analysis of key-pair-superposition generation) First by Theorem
6.1
we could assume the state after the first step of the protocol is within Setup
1
. Denote the purified joint state as
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
.

2.

(Analysis of the first
{\mathsf{StdBTest}}
) Since the standard basis test is executed on
\ket{\varphi^{1}}
with probability
1/2
, by (
329
) the server has to be able to make the client output
{\mathsf{pass}}
in this test with probability
\geq 1-2\times 10^{-2000}
. That allows us to apply Lemma
6.2
and get

\exists\text{ efficient server-side operator }\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.sbt}:\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.sbt}\ket{\varphi^{1}}\text{ is $1.5\times 10^{-1000}$-basis-honest for $({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}})$}

(330)

Denote

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}}:=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.sbt}\ket{\varphi^{1}}

(331)

3.

(Analysis of the switch gadget technique) Denote the output state of
{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}
as
\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}
:

\displaystyle\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}:=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.a}}(({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}}),{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{1}}

(332)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.a}\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.sbt}^{-1}}(({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}}),{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}}

(333)

where
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.a}
is the
{\mathsf{Adv}}
’s operation in step 2.a of the verifiable state preparation case in Protocol
2
.

Since the verifiable state preparation case is reached with probability
\frac{1}{2}
, together with (
329
) we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}|^{2}\geq 1-2\times 10^{-2000}

That allows us to apply Theorem
8.1
. As Setup
3
, define
H^{\prime}
as the blinded version of
H
where entries
\{0,1\}^{\kappa}||K^{({\text{switch}})}||\cdots
are blinded. Define
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{\geq 2.b}
as the blinded version of
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}
(the part of
{\mathsf{Adv}}
starting from the step
b
of the verifiable state preparation) where each random oracle query is replaced by a query to
H^{\prime}
. Then we have

{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}\approx_{15\times 10^{-1000}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}^{blind}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}

(334)

where
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
denotes the execution of Protocol
2
starting from the 2.b step.

We will analyze the right hand side of (
334
) to understand the left hand side.

4.

(Analysis of the second
{\mathsf{StdBTest}}
) First by (
334
)(
329
) we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}^{blind}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}|^{2}\geq 1-16\times 10^{-1000}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(335)

Recall
{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}
is defined as the set of adversaries that only query the blinded oracle
H^{\prime}
. First in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
a standard basis test is executed with probability
\frac{1}{5}
. This together with (
335
) by Theorem
6.2
implies

\exists\text{ efficient server-side operator }\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}:\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}\text{ is $(9\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))$-basis-honest for ${\boldsymbol{K}}$}

(336)

5.

(Analysis of
{\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}
) Define

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}:=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}

(337)

Now
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}
is in Setup
3
.
Applying Theorem
9.1
and Fact
9
we have

{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}\approx^{ind:{\mathcal{F}}_{cq\land blind}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}

(338)

6.

(Analysis of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
) Now the left hand side of (
338
) is in Setup
4
. From (
335
)(
337
)(
338
) and the fact that
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
is executed with probability
\frac{1}{5}
we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}\circ(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}))|^{2}\geq 1-80\times 10^{-1000}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(339)

where
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}^{blind}
is the part of
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{\geq 2.b}
in the
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
step. Apply Theorem
11.3
and we get

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}\circ(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}))|^{2}\leq({\text{OPT}}+10^{-240}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))

(340)

Now we start from (
340
) and substituting these analysis back step-by-step.
Substituting (
338
), we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{2.a}})|^{2}\leq({\text{OPT}}+10^{-240}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))

(341)

Since
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
is executed with
\frac{1}{5}
probability in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
, this implies

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}_{\geq 2.b}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{2.a}})|^{2}\leq({\text{OPT}}+10^{-240}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))\cdot\frac{1}{5}

(342)

Substituting (
334
):

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{2.a}})|^{2}\leq({\text{OPT}}+1.01\times 10^{-240}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))\cdot\frac{1}{5}

(343)

Since
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
is executed with
\frac{1}{2}
probability in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
, we get

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{0}}|\leq({\text{OPT}}+1.01\times 10^{-240}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))\cdot\frac{1}{10}

(344)

which completes the proof.
∎

13.2
A Proof of Theorem
5.2

In this section we prove Theorem
5.2
, the verifiability property of
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
. The proof is divided into three parts: (1) First we analyze each step of the protocol; (2) then we formally construct the isometry; (3) finally we prove the isometry satisfies Definition
4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Suppose the initial purified joint state is
\ket{\varphi^{0}}=O\ket{0}
as described in Definition
4.6
. Consider an efficient adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
whose corresponding final output state is

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}:={\mathsf{preRSPV}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{0}}

Suppose the passing probability and the winning probability are big:

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}|^{2}>1-10^{-2000}

(345)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}|^{2}>({\text{OPT}}-10^{-200})\cdot p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}=({\text{OPT}}-10^{-200})\cdot\frac{1}{10}

(346)

Our goal is to give an isometry
{\mathsf{Sim}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}
that satisfies (
70
).

Part I: analysis of the protocol

To achieve this goal, we first need to analyze the protocol step-by-step to understand
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}
.

This part of the proof is as below. The first five steps are the same as the proof of Theorem
5.1
; we give a summary for the important conclusion in each step.

1-5.

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{1}}:=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.sbt}\ket{\varphi^{1}}\text{is $1.5\times 10^{-1000}$-basis-honest for $({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}})$.}

(347)

\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}:={\mathsf{SwPhaseUpdate}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.a}}(({\boldsymbol{K}}^{({\text{switch}})},{\boldsymbol{K}}),{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{1}}

(348)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}|^{2}\geq 1-2\times 10^{-2000}

{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}\approx_{1.5\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}^{blind}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}

(349)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{\geq 2.b}^{blind}}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}|^{2}\geq 1-3.1\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(350)

\exists\text{ efficient server-side operator }\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{blind}:\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}\text{ is $(9\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))$-basis-honest for ${\boldsymbol{K}}$}

(351)

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}:=\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind}\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}

{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}\approx^{ind:{\mathcal{F}}_{cq\land blind}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}

(352)

6.

(Analysis of
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
) By (
350
) and the fact that
{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}
is executed with probability
\frac{1}{5}
in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi^{2.a}}|^{2}\geq 1-16\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

Thus by (
352
)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{CoPhTest}}}^{blind}\circ(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa}){\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})|^{2}\geq 1-16\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

where
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})
is in Setup
4
. We can further randomize
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}
under
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
by Theorem
10.3
:

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))\approx_{10^{-123}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})

(353)

7.

(Analysis of
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
) Finally we analyze the
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
. As the previous step, we know
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})
is in Setup
4
. By (
350
)(
352
) and the fact that
{\mathsf{InPhTest}}
is executed with probability
\frac{1}{5}
in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}_{\geq 2.b}
we know the following about the passing probability and winning probability:

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}^{blind}\circ(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa}){\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})|^{2}>1-16\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{win}}}{\mathsf{InPhTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{InPhTest}}}^{blind}\circ(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{K}},{\boldsymbol{\Theta}};1^{\kappa}){\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})|^{2}>{\text{OPT}}-16\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

By Theorem
11.6
we can further randomize the state under
{\mathcal{P}}
:

{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))\approx_{10^{-31}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})

(354)

8.

(A temporary summary and preparation) (
352
)(
353
)(
354
) implies

{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))\approx_{1.1\times 10^{-31}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}\approx^{ind:{\mathcal{F}}_{cq\land blind}}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}

(355)

We aim at giving a server-side isomorphism between the left hand side of (
355
) and the target state (
67
).

Let’s first analyze the state in the left hand side of (
355
). By Theorem
10.2

{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\circ\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))
is a pre-phase-honest form. Recall the indicator qubit registers used by
{\mathcal{P}}
are denoted by
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
. The operation of
{\mathcal{P}}
extracts the information of whether the state phases are under complex conjugation to these indicator registers, and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
erases the values in the indicator registers. Below we want to make this information explicit thus we first work under
{\mathcal{P}}
instead of
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
. Besides that, we also want to treat the
{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(0)}
key pair separately.

By Lemma
11.5
we have, there exist states
\ket{\phi_{0,+}}
,
\ket{\phi_{0,-}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1,+}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1,-}}
such that:

\displaystyle\forall b^{(0)}\in\{0,1\}:\quad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}^{(0)}_{bsh}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{b^{(0)}}}{\sf ReviseRO}\circ\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{2.a}})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}\underbrace{\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},+}}+\ket{{\mathsf{false}}}\ket{{\mathsf{true}}}\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},-}}

(356)

and for each
b^{(0)}\in\{0,1\}
,
\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},+}}
,
\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},-}}
have the following form, for some state family

(\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},+}},\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},-}})_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}}),b^{(0)}\in\{0,1\},\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}
:

\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},+}}:=\underbrace{\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}}_{\text{client}}\otimes\underbrace{\ket{x_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}e^{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}}_{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}}\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},+}}

(357)

\ket{\phi_{b^{(0)},-}}:=\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}}\otimes\sum_{\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}e^{-{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},-}}

(358)

For each
b^{(0)}\in\{0,1\}
,
\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}
, denote the overall norms of the
\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\vec{b}}
-branch of these states as
c_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},+},c_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},-}
:

c_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},+}=|\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},+}}|

(359)

c_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},-}=|\sum_{K\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{K}\otimes\sum_{\Theta\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{\Theta}})}\ket{\Theta}\otimes\ket{x_{b^{(0)}}^{(0)}}\otimes e^{-{\text{SUM}}(\vec{\Theta}_{\vec{b}}){\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\vec{x}_{\vec{b}}}\ket{\phi_{K,b^{(0)},\vec{b},-}}|

(360)

Now the remaining thing is to derive properties of these norms by analyzing
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
.

9.

(Analysis of
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
) By (
350
) and the fact that
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
is executed with probability
\frac{1}{5}
, we know

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}|^{2}>1-16\times 10^{-500}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

\Rightarrow|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}|^{2}<40\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

Apply the collapsing property (Lemma
6.3
) on
{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}
, use
\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{0}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}
,
\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{1}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}
to denote the projection onto the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}
-branch and
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}^{(0)}
-branch, we have

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{0}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}|^{2}<40\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(361)

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{1}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}}|^{2}<40\times 10^{-500}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(362)

Now we can analyze each of (
361
)(
362
) to derive basis norm relations for
b^{(0)}=0,1
. But to get what we want on (
359
)(
360
), we need to separate the honest phase part and the complex-conjugated phase part, as in (
356
).

Without loss of generality let’s first consider (
361
) and use it to analyze (
359
)(
360
) for
b^{(0)}=0
. To achieve it, we first substitute (
355
) to (
361
) and get

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})

\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{0}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\circ\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))))|^{2}<1.2\times 10^{-31}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(363)

Recall the definition of
{\mathcal{P}}
in Definition
11.1
, there is:

{\mathcal{P}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}}=(\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}+\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+}}_{{\mathsf{false}}}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}}_{{\mathsf{true}}}){\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}}

Note that with only the
{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0}^{(0)}
-branch,
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+},{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
are equivalent to local operators that only apply on the
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}^{(0)}
,
{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{+},{\boldsymbol{indic}}_{-}
registers. But
{\mathsf{BUTest}}
does not take these registers as inputs. And
\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{0,+},{\mathcal{P}}_{0,-}
also commute, which implies (
363
) could be further simplified by replacing the
{\mathcal{P}}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}
in it by
{\mathcal{P}}
:

|\Pi_{{\mathsf{fail}}}{\mathsf{BUTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{{\mathsf{BUTest}}}^{blind}(\tilde{\mathsf{Adv}}_{2.b.sbt}^{blind})^{-1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})

\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(0)}_{0}}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{bsh}^{(0)}}{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{R}}_{2}(\ket{\$_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{R}}_{1}(\ket{\$_{1}}\otimes{\sf ReviseRO}\circ\Pi_{{\text{basishonest}}({\boldsymbol{K}})}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{2.a}})))))|^{2}<1.2\times 10^{-31}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(364)

which could be further decomposed into

This step is a preparation for dealing with the blinded oracle later. Recall that when we analyze the final outcome state

, one important step is to use (

For the simulated state, we also need a way to connect the adversary that queries the normal oracle to an adversary that query the blinded oracle. This problem is addressed in this step, by artificially swapping the values of normal oracle and the blinded oracle in
\ket{\chi_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},\pm}^{1}}

Define

)(

{\boldsymbol{H}}^{\prime}
registers that originally hold the blinded part, with

What we get is the following. Suppose

|\Pi_{0}{\mathcal{U}}\ket{\chi_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},\pm}^{2}}|^{2}=|\Pi_{0}{\mathcal{U}}^{blind}\ket{\chi_{b^{(0)},\vec{b},\pm}^{1}}|^{2}\text{ for all $\pm\in\{+,-\},b^{(0)}\in\{0,1\},\vec{b}\in\{0,1\}^{L}$}

24

14
From Remote State Preparation to Quantum Computation Verification

In this section we complete the construction of our CVQC protocol thus complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

14.1
From Pre-RSPV to RSPV

In this subsection we will give an RSPV protocol (as defined in Definition
4.2
,
4.3
) from the pre-RSPV protocol (as defined in Definition
4.4
,
4.5
, constructed in Protocol
2
).

Comparing the definition of RSPV to pre-RSPV, the differences are:

•

In Definition
4.5
there is an additional case in the conclusion that the winning probability is bounded away from
OPT
. This means in
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
the adversary could possibly cheat by making the winning probability small.

In addition, in Definition
4.3
the bound on the passing probability in the first case is much smaller than Definition
4.5
. This means in
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
the adversary’s freedom of cheating without being caught is much smaller.

•

In
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
the output state is only generated in the
{\mathsf{comp}}
round, which appears with probability
p_{{\mathsf{comp}}}
; in
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
the state should be generated with high probability.

We do the security amplification from
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
to
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
in two steps, as follows.

1.

{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
to
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
:

(a)

In Step 1, both parties do a repetition of
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
protocol. And the client calculates the number of winning cases. If it’s significantly fewer than the optimal expectation value, the client outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
. (The client also outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
if any call to the subprotocols
{\mathsf{fail}}
.)

In this way we resolve the problems discussed in the first bullet above. For the second bullet we put it into the second step below, and for the honest behavior (correctness property) of this step we use a simple solution as follows:

(b)

The client chooses a random index
i
and reveals it. If the
i
-th round is a
{\mathsf{comp}}
round, then the server gets the state and the client stores
{\mathsf{comp}}
as the overall flag. Otherwise the client stores
\perp
as the overall round type.

Thus
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
will have the following outputs: type
\in\{{\mathsf{comp}},\perp\}
, flag
\in\{{\mathsf{pass}},{\mathsf{fail}}\}
, and the client-side keys and server-side states (only if type
={\mathsf{comp}}
).

2.

{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
to
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
:

Both parties repeat the
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
protocol for many times to ensure an output state is generated with high probability.

14.1.1
Step 1: a fully verifiable protocol that does not necessarily generate an output state

We first construct
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
from
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
.

Protocol 14

(
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
)
.

Suppose the security parameter is
\kappa
and the output number is
L
.

1.

Take the round number
N=10^{2500}
.

For
i
in
[N]
:

(a)

Both parties run protocol
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})
.

Note for each
i
there is a round type sampled from
\{{\mathsf{test}},{\mathsf{quiz}},{\mathsf{comp}}\}
. In addition to the
{\mathsf{pass}}/{\mathsf{fail}}
flag, the client will output a score in
\{{\mathsf{win}},{\mathsf{lose}},\perp\}
, where
{\mathsf{win}}/{\mathsf{lose}}
only appear in the
{\mathsf{quiz}}
round, and honestly,
score={\mathsf{win}}
with probability
OPT
(conditioned on
{\mathsf{quiz}}
round).

2.

If any round
{\mathsf{fail}}
, the client outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
.

If the total number of
{\mathsf{win}}
is
\leq N\cdot p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}\cdot({\text{OPT}}-10^{-210})
(recall
{\text{OPT}}=\frac{1}{3}\cos^{2}(\pi/8)=0.28451779686\cdots
,
p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}=\frac{1}{10}
), the client outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
.

3.

The client picks a random round
i
and tells
i
to the server.

•

If the round type of the
i
-th round is
{\mathsf{comp}}
, the server picks up the corresponding gadgets and disgards the others. The client keeps the keys, outputs
{\mathsf{comp}}
in the overall round type register and disgards the other systems.

•

If the round type of the
i
-th round is not
{\mathsf{comp}}
, the client stores
\perp
in the overall round type register. Both parties disgard everything else.

Correctness

In the honest setting conditioned on the overall round type is
{\mathsf{comp}}
, with probability
\geq 1-10^{-5}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
the joint output state of the client and the server is (
66
).

Proof of correctness.

By Section
5.4
in the honest setting each call to
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
could
{\mathsf{fail}}
only with negligible probability. Thus except with negligible probability, the only case where the client will output
{\mathsf{fail}}
against an honest server is the statistical testing of
{\mathsf{quiz}}
scores (number of
{\mathsf{win}}
) in the second step. By properties of the
{\mathsf{preRSPV}}
protocol the honest server could generate a
{\mathsf{win}}
score with probability
\geq{\text{OPT}}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
in each
{\mathsf{quiz}}
round. Thus the expectation of
{\mathsf{win}}
for each round
i\in[N]
is
\geq p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}\cdot{\text{OPT}}-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
. Then by Chernoff’s bound

\Pr[\text{ the number of ${\mathsf{win}}$ is $\leq N\cdot p_{{\mathsf{quiz}}}\cdot({\text{OPT}}-10^{-210})$}]\leq 10^{-6}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

This completes the proof.
∎

Denote the final state as

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}}=\sum_{rec^{<i}\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{rec}}^{<i})}\ket{rec^{<i}}\otimes{\mathsf{Sim}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{rec^{<i},>i}}_{rec^{<i},>i}\ket{\tilde{\varphi}^{i}_{rec^{<i}}}

14.1.2
Step 2: handing the case where
{\mathsf{comp}}
round is not reached

In this section we construct
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
protocol from the
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
protocol in the last section.

Protocol 15

(
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
)
.

Inputs: security parameter
\kappa
, output number
L
.

Take
N=10/p_{{\mathsf{comp}}}=100
.

1.

For
i\in[N]
:

(a)

Both parties run
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
.

•

If this subprotocol call outputs
{\mathsf{comp}}
in its round type register, break out of the loop.

•

Otherwise both the client and the server disgard all outputs (client side phases and server-side states) of this round.

2.

If the loop in the first step does not terminate by the breaking out command, the client outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
in the flag register. If any subprotocol call returns
{\mathsf{fail}}
as its flag, the client outputs
{\mathsf{fail}}
too.

Otherwise, suppose the first step breaks out when the round counter is
i
. The client and the server use the keys and states generated in the
i
-th round as the output keys and the output state.

Correctness

If the server is honest, the protocol succeeds with probability
\geq 0.98-{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
and the joint state of the client and the server is (
66
) (up to negligible distance) in the end.

Proof.

The cases where an honest server could result in
{\mathsf{fail}}
are:

•

In the underlying
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
protocol the client could possibly output
{\mathsf{fail}}
against an honest server. This happens with probability
\leq N\cdot(10^{-4}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa))\leq 0.01+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
.

•

The probability that all the calls of
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
have round type
\perp
is
\leq(1-p_{{\mathsf{comp}}})^{N}\leq 0.01
.

If these two cases do not happen, the client and the honest server get the output keys and states (
66
) by the correctness of
{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}
. This completes the proof.∎

Then we could prove Protocol
15
satisfies Definition
4.3
, the verifiability requirement for
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
protocol:

Theorem 14.2

(Verifiability of Protocol
15
, repeat of Definition
4.3
)
.

For any efficient adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
there exists a server-side operator
{\mathsf{Sim}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}}
such that:

\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{0}\approx^{ind}_{0.15+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{Sim}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}\ket{\text{Equation \eqref{eq:target}}}

(448)

Proof.

Expand the left hand side of (
448
) by the value of the register that stores the round counter
i
when the protocol terminates:

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{0}

(449)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[N]}\underbrace{\ket{i}}_{\text{round counter}}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}\Pi_{{\mathsf{comp}}}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{i}}{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{i}}(\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}}\ket{0})

(450)

\displaystyle+\ket{N}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{i}}{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{i}}(\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}}\ket{0})

(451)

where
\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}
is the projection onto the space that the flag registers for round
1
to
i-1
all have value
{\mathsf{pass}}
,
\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}
is the projection onto the space that the round type registers for round

to

all have value
\perp
,
{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}
is the protocol from round
1
to
i-1
,
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}
is the part of
{\mathsf{Adv}}
by the end of the
i-1
-th round. And
\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}
,
\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{i}}
,
{\mathsf{Adv}}_{i}
are defined similarly.

The norm of term (
451
) is upper bounded by
(1-p_{{\mathsf{comp}}})^{N}\leq 0.01
. Thus

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:258}\approx_{0.01}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[N]}\ket{i}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}\Pi_{{\mathsf{comp}}}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{i}}{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{i}}(\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}}\ket{0})

(452)

Then we could apply Theorem
14.1
to each term of (

) which leads to an isometry
{\mathsf{Sim}}^{i,{\mathsf{Adv}}}
for each
i\in[N]
:

\displaystyle\forall i\in[N],\quad

\displaystyle\ket{i}\otimes\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}\Pi_{{\mathsf{comp}}}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{i}}{\mathsf{preRSPVTemp}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{i}}(\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}}\ket{0})

(453)

\displaystyle\approx^{ind}_{0.11\sqrt{p_{i}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

\displaystyle\ket{i}\otimes\sqrt{p_{i}}\sqrt{p_{{\mathsf{comp}}}}\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{i}}{\mathsf{Sim}}^{i,{\mathsf{Adv}}}\ket{\text{Equation \eqref{eq:target}}}

(454)

where

p_{i}:=|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}^{{\boldsymbol{flag}}_{<i}}\Pi_{\perp}^{{\boldsymbol{type}}_{<i}}{\mathsf{RSPV}}_{<i}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}_{<i}}\ket{0}|^{2}

Then summing them up and summing up the error terms we could continue from (

Right hand side of
(

)

(455)

\displaystyle\approx^{ind}_{0.11+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}

14.2
From RSPV to CVQC

Now we will construct a CVQC protocol from the RSPV protocol in the last subsection.

Protocol 16

(CVQC)
.

Input: circuit
C
to be verified, which determines the gadget number
L=O(|C|)
under Theorem
4.2
. Suppose the security parameter is
\kappa
.

1.

Both parties run
{\mathsf{RSPV}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})
.

2.

Both parties run the gadget-assisted verification protocol (Theorem
4.2
) with the gadgets above.

We have the following for the protocol, which proves Theorem
1.1
.

Completeness

The protocol has completeness
\frac{2}{3}
.

Proof.

There are two cases where the client in the protocol will output
{\mathsf{fail}}
on a yes instance against an honest server:

•

The call to the
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
protocol in the first step might fail with probability
\frac{1}{10}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
, as defined in Definition
4.2
.

•

The underlying gadget-assisted verification protocol in the third step is allowed to have a failing probability of
\frac{1}{10}
.

The total probability is
\leq\frac{1}{3}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)
. ∎

Soundness

The protocol has soundness
\frac{1}{3}
in QROM against BQP adversaries.

Proof.

After the application of
{\mathsf{RSPV}}
against adversary
{\mathsf{Adv}}
, define
\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}
as the output state:

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}={\mathsf{RSPV}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}(1^{L},1^{\kappa})\ket{0}

By Theorem
14.2
, there exists a server-side isometry
{\mathsf{Sim}}
such that

\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}}\approx_{0.15+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}^{ind}\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{Sim}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}\ket{\text{Equation \eqref{eq:target}}}

(457)

By the soundness property of the underlying gadget-assisted protocol (by Theorem
4.2
, denote as
{\mathsf{GAUVBQC}}
) we know for any
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}
which denotes the adversary for the gadget-assisted verification step:

\forall C,o,\Pr[C\ket{0}=o]\leq\frac{1}{100}:|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{GAUVBQC}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}}(C,o,\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{Sim}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}\ket{\text{Equation \eqref{eq:target}}})|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{50}

Substitute (
457
) we get

\forall C,o,\Pr[C\ket{0}=o]\leq\frac{1}{100}:|\Pi_{{\mathsf{pass}}}{\mathsf{GAUVBQC}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}}(C,o,\ket{\varphi^{\prime}})|^{2}\leq 0.33+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

which completes the proof.
∎

Efficiency

The protocol runs in time
O({\mathsf{poly}}(\kappa)|C|)
.

By this time we complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

.

Proof of Fact
10
.

This is because state

\frac{1}{\sqrt{8}}\sum_{\theta\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}\ket{\theta}

is invariant under the operator.
∎

Proof of Fact
11
.

This is because for any
sum\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}
, state

\sum_{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}\in{\mathcal{C}},{\text{SUM}}(c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N})=sum}\ket{c_{0}c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{N}}

Proof of Fact
4
.

Suppose
\vec{c}=(c_{1},c_{2}\cdots c_{D})
,
\vec{d}=(d_{1},d_{2}\cdots d_{D})
. Denote
S
as the set of index
i
such that
c_{i}\leq d_{i}
.
Then

\vec{c^{\prime}}\approx_{\epsilon_{1}}\vec{d}\Rightarrow\sum_{i\in S}|c_{i}-d_{i}|^{2}\leq\epsilon_{1}^{2}

\vec{c}\approx_{\epsilon_{2}}\vec{d^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\sum_{i\not\in S}|c_{i}-d_{i}|^{2}\leq\epsilon_{2}^{2}

Summing them up completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Fact
5
.

\frac{1}{{|D|}}\sum_{d_{1}\in D}\sum_{d_{2}\in D}|c_{d_{1}}-c_{d_{2}}|^{2}=2\sum_{d\in D}(1+\frac{1}{D})c_{d}^{2}-2(\frac{1}{\sqrt{|D|}}\sum_{d\in D}c_{d})^{2}\approx_{\epsilon}0

\Rightarrow\frac{1}{\sqrt{|D|}}\sum_{d\in D}c_{d}\approx_{\frac{3}{2}\epsilon/c+\frac{1}{D}}c

\Rightarrow\sum_{d\in D}|c_{d}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{D}}c|^{2}=c^{2}-2(\frac{1}{\sqrt{|D|}}\sum_{d\in D}c_{d})c+c^{2}\approx_{4\epsilon/c+\frac{2}{D}}0

∎

Proof of Fact
6
.

Define
\tilde{\mathsf{Sim}}
as the operation that runs
O
and measures register
{\boldsymbol{C}}
until
c
appears. Cuting-off the repetition in
\tilde{\mathsf{Sim}}
by
\kappa
and purifying the operation completes the construction.
∎

Lemma
3.1
is the usual Chernoff’s bound.

Proof of Corollary
3.2
.

We only need to prove

\Pr[(\text{$\forall i$, sample history by time $i$ is not in $S_{i}$})\land(|\{i:s_{i}=1\}|\geq(1+\delta)pN)]\leq e^{-\delta^{2}N/4}

which comes from Lemma
3.1
.
∎

A proof of Lemma
3.4
is given in
[
61
]
using standard techniques. We give a proof here for self-containment.

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Define
\Pi
as the projection onto strings with prefix in
{\boldsymbol{pads}}
in the query input register. Then consider
H(\mathbb{I}-\Pi)
which is the operation that projecting out the space that have a prefix in
{\boldsymbol{pads}}
from the query.

Define
O_{k}
as the operation that the last
k
queries of
O
to the random oracle are replaced by
H(\mathbb{I}-\Pi)
.

We can prove, for all
k\in[|O|]
(
|O|
denotes the number of queries) :

O_{k}\sum_{pads\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})|}}\ket{pads}\otimes\ket{\varphi}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}O_{k-1}\sum_{pads\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})|}}\ket{pads}\otimes\ket{\varphi}.

(458)

The reason is, (
458
) could be reduced to

|\Pi\sum_{pads\in{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\text{Domain}}({\boldsymbol{pads}})|}}\ket{pads}\otimes U_{k}\ket{\varphi}|\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}0

(459)

where
U
is the part of
O
just before the
k
-th query counting from the last to the first. Note by the time of the
k
-th query (counting from the last to the first)
pads
still contains unused freshly new randomness. Thus (
459
) is true by direct calculation.

Summing up (
458
) for each
k\in[|O|]
. Then
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}:=O_{|O|}\ket{\varphi}
gives the state we want.
∎

Proof of Lemma

.

Substitute

and expand we get

|\Pi_{0}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}}O\ket{\varphi}|^{2}=|\Pi_{0}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}}O\ket{\varphi_{0}}|^{2}+|\Pi_{0}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}}O\ket{\varphi_{1}}|^{2}+\bra{\varphi_{1}}O^{\dagger}\Pi_{0}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}}O\ket{\varphi_{0}}+\bra{\varphi_{0}}O^{\dagger}\Pi_{0}^{{\boldsymbol{S}}}O\ket{\varphi_{1}}

463
) we know

|\Pi_{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1-b}^{(i)}}{\mathsf{Adv}}^{\prime}(\ket{\varphi}\odot\llbracket{\mathsf{AuxInf}}\rrbracket)|\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

which together with (
464
) completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

Define
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{t}
as the operation where the first
t
queries of
{\mathsf{Adv}}
are replaced by queries to
H^{\prime}
. The problem is reduced to

{\mathsf{Adv}}^{t}\ket{\varphi}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathsf{Adv}}^{t-1}\ket{\varphi}

as the proof of Lemma
6.4
, this is further reduced to

|\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{S}}_{roq}}_{\cdots||{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1-b}^{(i)}||\cdots}U^{t-1}\ket{\varphi}|\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

which holds by claw-freeness.
∎

, we first prove the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose the client holds a key pair in register
{\boldsymbol{K}}=\{{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}\}
. Below we use
|{\boldsymbol{x}}|

{\boldsymbol{K}}
. Suppose the purified joint state

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}=\sum_{pad\in\{0,1\}^{\kappa}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{\kappa}}}\underbrace{\ket{pad}}_{{\boldsymbol{pad}}\text{ in transcript}}\otimes\ket{\tilde{\varphi}_{pad}}

H({\boldsymbol{pad}}||\{0,1\}^{|{\boldsymbol{x}}|})

|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}_{\geq 2}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}_{\geq 2}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|

(465)

where the subscript “

” means the first step of
{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}
(sampling a random pad) has already been done and thus skipped.

The proof is by a direct calculation similar to the proof of Lemma A.7.1 in

]
.

Proof.

b=0
. Since

\text{${\mathsf{HadamardTest}}_{\geq 2}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}$ does not depend on ${\boldsymbol{H}}({\boldsymbol{pad}}||{\boldsymbol{x}}_{0})$}.

(466)

Then we can expand the left hand side of (

) as follows:

\displaystyle|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}_{\geq 2}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|

(471)

∎

Then we could prove Lemma

.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We will move towards Lemma
C.1
step by step.

1.

Suppose the random pad used in the Hadamard test is stored in register
{\boldsymbol{pad}}
. By Lemma
3.4
there exists
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}
such that

\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{\kappa}}}\sum_{pad\in\{0,1\}^{\kappa}}\underbrace{\ket{pad}}_{{\boldsymbol{pad}}}\otimes\ket{\varphi}

and
\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}
does not depend on the value of
{\boldsymbol{H}}({\boldsymbol{pad}}||\cdots)
.

2.

By Lemma
6.5
we have

{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi}

where
{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}
come from replacing all the queries in
{\mathsf{Adv}}
by queries to
{\boldsymbol{H}}^{\prime}
, where
{\boldsymbol{H}}^{\prime}
is the blinded version of
{\boldsymbol{H}}
where entries
\{0,1\}^{\kappa}||{\boldsymbol{K}}
are blinded.

3.

We can apply Lemma
C.1
to get

|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}_{\geq 2}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}_{\geq 2}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|

|\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}_{\geq 2}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{{\mathsf{Adv}}^{blind}}_{\geq 2}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\tilde{\varphi}}|

Combining all these steps completes the proof.
∎

Then we could prove Corollary
7.3
from this lemma.

Proof of Corollary
7.3
.

For each
b\in\{0,1\}
, define

\ket{\varphi_{b,0}^{\prime}}:=\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=0}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi_{b}}

\ket{\varphi_{b,1}^{\prime}}:=\Pi_{\eqref{eq:htt}=1}^{{\boldsymbol{d}}}\Pi_{\neq 0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi_{b}}

\ket{\varphi_{b,-}^{\prime}}:=\Pi_{=0}^{\text{last $\kappa$ bits of }{\boldsymbol{d}}}{\mathsf{HadamardTest}}^{\mathsf{Adv}}({\boldsymbol{K}};1^{\kappa})\ket{\varphi_{b}}

Then by Lemma
7.2
we have

\forall b\in\{0,1\},|\ket{\varphi_{b,0}^{\prime}}|\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\ket{\varphi_{b,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,1}}|,|\ket{\varphi_{b,1}^{\prime}}|\approx_{{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\ket{\varphi_{b,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{b,1}}|

(472)

From the condition of Corollary
7.3
we get

|\ket{\varphi_{0,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,0}^{\prime}}|\geq\sqrt{1-p}-\epsilon

(473)

On the other hand

\displaystyle|\ket{\varphi_{0,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,0}^{\prime}}|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\sqrt{|\ket{\varphi_{0,0}^{\prime}}|^{2}+|\ket{\varphi_{1,0}^{\prime}}|^{2}}

(474)

\displaystyle\text{(By \eqref{eq:266})}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{|\ket{\varphi_{0,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi_{0,1}^{\prime}}|^{2}+|\ket{\varphi_{1,0}^{\prime}}+\ket{\varphi_{1,1}^{\prime}}|^{2}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(475)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-\epsilon^{2}-|\ket{\varphi_{0,-}^{\prime}}|^{2}-|\ket{\varphi_{1,-}^{\prime}}|^{2}}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

(476)

Comparing it with (
473
) we get

|\ket{\varphi_{0,-}^{\prime}}|^{2}+|\ket{\varphi_{1,-}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq p+2\epsilon+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

\Rightarrow\forall b\in\{0,1\},|\ket{\varphi_{b,-}^{\prime}}|\leq\sqrt{p+2\epsilon}+{\mathsf{negl}}(\kappa)

This completes the proof of (
110
).

For (
7.3
), we can first bound

|\ket{\varphi_{0,0}^{\prime}}|^{2}+|\ket{\varphi_{1,0}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}(|\ket{\varphi_{0}}|^{2}+|\ket{\varphi_{1}}|^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}(1-\epsilon^{2})

(477)

which together with (
473
) allows us to apply Fact
3
and get

\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,0}}\approx_{\sqrt{p+\epsilon}}\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,0}}

which completes the proof of (
7.3
).

Finally by the condition of Corollary
7.3
again we get, the norm of the failing space of the output state is
\leq\sqrt{p}
, thus

|\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{0,1}}+\ket{\varphi^{\prime}_{1,1}}|\leq\sqrt{p}+\sqrt{\epsilon}

which completes the proof.
∎
