Appendix E
Missing Proofs in Section
11.1

We will prove the lemmas in Section
11.1
step by step.

E.1
Basic Inequalities

Before going to the proofs, we give the following basic lemmas. These lemmas could be proved via basic linear algebra or calculus.

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose
\vec{a}=(a_{1},a_{2}\cdots a_{n})
,
\vec{b}=(b_{1},b_{2}\cdots b_{n})
. If
\frac{\vec{a}\cdot\vec{b}}{|\vec{a}||\vec{b}|}\geq 1-\epsilon
, there is

\vec{a}/|\vec{a}|\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}}\vec{b}/|\vec{b}|

Lemma E.2
.

If
\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos\lambda)+\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos(-\lambda))\approx_{\epsilon}2\cos^{2}(\pi/8)
,
\epsilon<0.1
, there is
\lambda\approx_{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}0
.

E.2
3
-states Lemmas

Below we use
{\operatorname{Re}}
to denote the real part of a complex number.

Lemma E.3
.

Suppose vectors
\ket{\varphi}
,
\ket{\psi}
both have norm
\leq 1
. Then for any
\ket{\chi}
with norm
\leq 1
,

|\ket{\varphi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}+|\ket{\psi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}\leq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\varphi|\psi}}{|\ket{\varphi}|\cdot|\ket{\psi}|}))

(479)

If
\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi}\neq 0
the equality holds iff
|\ket{\varphi}|=|\ket{\psi}|=1
and

\ket{\chi}=(\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi})/|\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi}|

(480)

Proof.

The left hand side of (
479
) is less than or equal to

4+2{\operatorname{Re}}(\braket{\varphi|\chi})+2{\operatorname{Re}}(\braket{\chi|\psi})

(481)

Up to an isometry, suppose
\ket{\varphi}=h\ket{0}
and
\ket{\psi}=(a+b{\mathrm{i}})\ket{0}+c\ket{1}
,
h,a,b,c\in\bbR_{\geq 0}
,
h\leq 1
,
a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}\leq 1
. Assume
\ket{\chi}=\alpha\ket{0}+\beta\ket{1}+\gamma\ket{2}
,
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\bC
,
|\alpha|^{2}+|\beta|^{2}+|\gamma|^{2}\leq 1
. Then we could calculate (
481
) directly by Cauchy’s inequality:

(
481
)

(482)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4+2((h+a){\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha)+b{\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha)+c{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))

(483)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}\sqrt{({\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))^{2}}

(484)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}

(485)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4+2\sqrt{h^{2}+2ah+1}

(486)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4+2\sqrt{2+2a}

(487)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\varphi|\psi}}{|\ket{\varphi}|}))

(488)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\varphi|\psi}}{|\ket{\varphi}|\cdot|\ket{\psi}|}))

(489)

where the equality holds when

h=1,a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}=1,({\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))^{2}=1

{\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha)/(h+a)={\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha)/b={\operatorname{Re}}(\beta)/c

This implies (
480
). ∎

Then we have the following lemma, which says the
\ket{\chi}
should be close to the optimal state if (
479
) holds approximately:

Lemma E.4
.

Suppose vectors
\ket{\varphi}
,
\ket{\psi}
both have norm
\leq 1
. Assume
30
30

30

This is to rule out the border case where
\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi}\approx 0
. In this case (
479
) still holds, but
\ket{\chi}
is not uniquely determined.

|\ket{\varphi}+\ket{\psi}|\geq 0.5
. If vector
\ket{\chi}
,
|\ket{\chi}|\leq 1
, satisfies

|\ket{\varphi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}+|\ket{\psi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}\geq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}((\frac{\braket{\varphi|\psi}}{|\ket{\varphi}|\cdot|\ket{\psi}|}))-\epsilon

(490)

where
\epsilon<0.1
. Then there is

\ket{\chi}\approx_{3\sqrt{\epsilon}}(\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi})/|\ket{\psi}+\ket{\varphi}|

(491)

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
E.3
we have

|\ket{\varphi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}+|\ket{\psi}+\ket{\chi}|^{2}=4+2((h+a){\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha)+b{\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha)+c{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))

where
h,\alpha,\beta,a,b,c
are defined in the same way.

Then (
482
)-(
489
) together with (
490
) imply all the inequalities in (
482
)-(
489
) are equality up to an error
\epsilon
, which means

4+2((h+a){\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha)+b{\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha)+c{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))\geq 4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}\sqrt{({\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))^{2}}-\epsilon

(492)

4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}\sqrt{({\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha))^{2}+({\operatorname{Re}}(\beta))^{2}}\geq 4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}-\epsilon

(493)

4+2\sqrt{(h+a)^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2}}\geq 4+2\sqrt{h^{2}+2ah+1}-\epsilon

(494)

4+2\sqrt{h^{2}+2ah+1}\geq 4+2\sqrt{2+2a}-\epsilon

(495)

By Lemma
E.1
, define

\vec{u}:=({\operatorname{Re}}(\alpha),{\operatorname{Im}}(\alpha),{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta)),\vec{v}:=(h+a,b,c),

the inequalities can be translated to

\text{(By condition) }|\vec{v}|=|\ket{\varphi}+\ket{\psi}|\geq 0.5

\text{(By \eqref{eq:426}) }\vec{u}\cdot\vec{v}\geq|\vec{u}|\cdot|\vec{v}|-\epsilon/2

\text{(By \eqref{eq:427}) }|\vec{u}|\geq 1-\epsilon/(2|\vec{v}|)\geq 1-\epsilon

(496)

thus

\frac{\vec{u}\cdot\vec{v}}{|\vec{u}|\cdot|\vec{v}|}\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{(1-\epsilon)}

By Lemma
E.1
there is

\vec{u}/|\vec{u}|\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon/(1-\epsilon)}}\vec{v}/|\vec{v}|

(497)

Note that by (
496
), and
|\ket{\chi}|\leq 1

\ket{\chi}\approx_{\sqrt{2\epsilon}}\alpha\ket{0}+{\operatorname{Re}}(\beta)\ket{1}

(498)

\vec{u}\approx_{1-1/(1-\epsilon)}\vec{u}/|\vec{u}|

(499)

Combining (
497
)(

)(

) implies (

).
∎

E.3
5
-states Lemmas, with Approximate Normalization

Corollary E.5
.

Suppose subnormalized vectors
\ket{\phi_{0}}
,
\ket{\phi_{4}}
satisfy
|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\approx_{\epsilon}1
,
|\ket{\phi_{4}}|\approx_{\epsilon}1
,
\ket{\phi_{0}}\approx_{\epsilon}-\ket{\phi_{4}}
,
\epsilon<0.1
. Then for subnormalized vectors
\ket{\phi_{1}}
,
\ket{\phi_{2}}
,
\ket{\phi_{3}}
there is

|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{1}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{3}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|^{2}\leq 16\cos^{2}(\pi/8)+6.6\epsilon

Proof.

By Lemma
E.3
:

|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{1}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|^{2}\leq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))

|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{3}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|^{2}\leq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{2}|\phi_{4}}}{|\ket{\phi_{2}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{4}}|}))

By basic calculus, from
\ket{\phi_{0}}/|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\approx_{3.3\epsilon}-\ket{\phi_{4}}/|\ket{\phi_{4}}|
we know

\displaystyle\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{2}|\phi_{4}}}{|\ket{\phi_{2}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{4}}|}))

(500)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(-\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+3.3\epsilon/4

(501)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cos^{2}(\pi/8)+3.3\epsilon/4

(502)

which completes the proof.∎

Corollary E.6
.

Suppose subnormalized vectors
\ket{\phi_{0}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1}}
,
\ket{\phi_{2}}
,
\ket{\phi_{3}}
,
\ket{\phi_{4}}
satisfy
|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\approx_{\epsilon}1
,
|\ket{\phi_{4}}|\approx_{\epsilon}1

\ket{\phi_{0}}\approx_{\epsilon}-\ket{\phi_{4}}
,
\epsilon<0.01
. If

|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{1}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{3}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|^{2}\geq 16\cos^{2}(\pi/8)-\epsilon

(503)

Then
\ket{\phi_{0}}
,
\ket{\phi_{1}}
,
\ket{\phi_{2}}
,
\ket{\phi_{3}}
,
\ket{\phi_{4}}
satisfy:

{\operatorname{Re}}(\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}})\approx_{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}0\text{ (that is, $|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|\approx_{8\sqrt{\epsilon}}\sqrt{2}$)}

(504)

{\operatorname{Re}}(\braket{\phi_{2}|\phi_{4}})\approx_{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}0\text{ (that is, $|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|\approx_{8\sqrt{\epsilon}}\sqrt{2}$)}

(505)

\ket{\phi_{1}}\approx_{15\sqrt{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{2}})

(506)

\ket{\phi_{3}}\approx_{15\sqrt{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{4}})

(507)

Proof.

. And

E.5
there is

\displaystyle|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{1}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{3}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|^{2}

(508)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{2}|\phi_{4}}}{|\ket{\phi_{2}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{4}}|}))

(509)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(-\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))+6.6\epsilon

\displaystyle 16\cos^{2}(\pi/8)+6.6\epsilon

(511)

which together with (

) implies each of (

)(

)(

)(

) are approximately

with error in

. First by (

):

which by Lemma
E.2
implies

{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|})\approx_{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}0

which proves (
504
); and by symmetry (
505
) is proved too.

And by the approximate equality of (
508
)(
509
) there is

|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{1}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|^{2}\geq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{0}|\phi_{2}}}{|\ket{\phi_{0}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{2}}|}))-7.6\epsilon

|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{3}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|^{2}\geq 8\cos^{2}(\frac{1}{4}\arccos{\operatorname{Re}}(\frac{\braket{\phi_{2}|\phi_{4}}}{|\ket{\phi_{2}}|\cdot|\ket{\phi_{4}}|}))-7.6\epsilon,

apply Lemma
E.4
we get

\ket{\phi_{1}}\approx_{9\sqrt{\epsilon}}(\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{2}})/|\ket{\phi_{0}}+\ket{\phi_{2}}|

\ket{\phi_{3}}\approx_{9\sqrt{\epsilon}}(\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{4}})/|\ket{\phi_{2}}+\ket{\phi_{4}}|

then substituting (
504
)(
505
) implies (
506
)(
507
).
∎

E.4
Proofs of Lemmas in Section
11.1

Now we are going to prove Lemma
11.1
, which is basically an application of Corollary
E.5
above.

Proof of Lemma
11.1
.

From the conditions we know

\forall i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}-\ket{\phi_{1,i+4}}\approx_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}-\ket{\phi_{0,i+4}}

Without loss of generality assume
A_{0}\leq A_{1}
. Apply Corollary
E.5
we have

|\ket{\phi_{0,0}}+\ket{\phi_{0,1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,1}}+\ket{\phi_{0,2}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,2}}+\ket{\phi_{0,3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,3}}+\ket{\phi_{0,4}}|^{2}\leq 2\cos^{2}(\frac{\pi}{8})A_{0}+5\sqrt{\epsilon}

|\ket{\phi_{0,4}}+\ket{\phi_{0,5}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,5}}+\ket{\phi_{0,6}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,6}}+\ket{\phi_{0,7}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,7}}+\ket{\phi_{0,0}}|^{2}\leq 2\cos^{2}(\frac{\pi}{8})A_{0}+5\sqrt{\epsilon}

which implies

\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i-1}}+\ket{\phi_{0,i}}|^{2}\leq 4\cos^{2}(\pi/8)A_{0}+10\sqrt{\epsilon}

which together with
\sum_{i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\}}|\ket{\phi_{0,i}}-\ket{\phi_{1,i}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon
completes the proof.
∎

Now we prove Lemma
11.2
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality assume
A_{0}\leq A_{1}
. From the conditions we know

\forall i\in\{0,1\cdots 7\},\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}-\ket{\phi_{1,i+4}}\approx_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}-\ket{\phi_{0,i+4}}

(512)

Substitute (
512
) to the fourth condition we get

|\ket{\phi_{0,0}}+\ket{\phi_{0,1}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,1}}+\ket{\phi_{0,2}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,2}}+\ket{\phi_{0,3}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,3}}+\ket{\phi_{0,4}}|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cos^{2}(\frac{\pi}{8})-17\epsilon

(513)

|\ket{\phi_{0,4}}+\ket{\phi_{0,5}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,5}}+\ket{\phi_{0,6}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,6}}+\ket{\phi_{0,7}}|^{2}+|\ket{\phi_{0,7}}+\ket{\phi_{0,0}}|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cos^{2}(\frac{\pi}{8})-17\epsilon

(514)

Apply Corollary
E.6
we have

\ket{\phi_{0,1}}\approx_{11\epsilon^{1/4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0,0}}+\ket{\phi_{0,2}})

(515)

\ket{\phi_{0,3}}\approx_{11\epsilon^{1/4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0,2}}+\ket{\phi_{0,4}})\approx_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0,2}}-\ket{\phi_{0,0}})

(516)

Define

\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,+}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0,0}}-{\mathrm{i}}\ket{\phi_{0,2}})

(517)

\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,-}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{\phi_{0,0}}+{\mathrm{i}}\ket{\phi_{0,2}})

(518)

Then through a direct calculation we can verify

\forall i\in\{0,1,2,3,4\},\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{12\epsilon^{1/4}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,+}}+e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,-}}

(519)

Which together with (
512
) we get

\forall i\in\{0,1,\cdots 7\},\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{13\epsilon^{1/4}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,+}}+e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,-}}

(520)

Thus for
\ket{\phi_{0,+}}
,
\ket{\phi_{0,-}}
defined in (
211
)(
212
), we have

\ket{\phi_{0,+}}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i\in\{0,1,\cdots 7\}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{13\epsilon^{1/4}}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,+}}

(521)

\ket{\phi_{0,-}}:=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i\in\{0,1,\cdots 7\}}e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{13\epsilon^{1/4}}\ket{\tilde{\phi}_{0,-}}

(522)

Combining it with (
520
) we have

\forall i\in\{0,1,\cdots 7\},\ket{\phi_{0,i}}\approx_{38\epsilon^{1/4}}e^{-i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,+}}+e^{i{\mathrm{i}}\pi/4}\ket{\phi_{0,-}}

Which together with (
512
) completes the proof of Lemma
11.2
.
∎
